What an application does with that fact is already bound — `is_content_lost` and the three `*_subscribe_content_lost` routes. Letting a caller *raise* the loss would let an application lie to the framework about the device, so these are `not-applicable` under the existing inward-direction theme.

**Three rows were already covered and nobody had noticed.** The explicit-state `SpriteBatch::Begin` overloads the merge added are expressed by `cna_sprite_batch_begin_with_effect` exactly as they stand: the route `CBIND-044A` wrote already takes all four state objects, the effect and the transform as nullable arguments, and the canonical overloads differ from one another only in which trailing arguments they carry. Established by reading the declaration, not assumed.

The rest: `Effect`'s protected copy constructor is what `Clone()` calls, so it maps to `cna_effect_clone`; `VertexBuffer::SetData<TVertex>` maps to `cna_vertex_buffer_set_data`, the same operation with the element type selected by a versioned descriptor rather than a template parameter (the `IPackedVectorT` flattening again); and `TextureCube`'s copy constructor and copy assignment are `not-applicable`, because the canonical copy is a value wrapper sharing one resource and in this ABI **the handle already is that shared reference** — minting a second name for it would create a second owner the registry must reconcile at destroy.

**Every test this row cites was checked to exist and to call the route it is cited for**, after a first draft named files from memory. 96/96 and all seven gates green; exports unchanged at 2,942, which is what a slice that adds no routes should do. |
| CBIND-081 | Bind the math tail | 7 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26**, and like `CBIND-080` it needed no new routes — the row called these "the cheapest rows in the phase, and the ones most likely to be quietly skipped as obvious", and the reason they are cheap is that C already expresses them.

`Vector2`'s six compound-assignment operators map to `cna_vector2_add`, `_subtract`, `_multiply`, `_multiply_scalar`, `_divide` and `_divide_scalar`, one per overload. C has neither operator overloading nor compound assignment on a struct, so `a += b` is the binary route with the destination naming the left operand — and that is well defined rather than an aliasing hazard precisely because every one of those routes takes its vectors **by value**. Checked, not assumed.

`Color::Color()` sets `packedValue` to 0, so its C form is the zero-initialized `CNA_Color` a caller writes in a declaration, or reaches through `cna_color_set_packed_value` with 0. A `cna_color_init` route would add a second spelling for something C already has syntax for. |
| CBIND-082 | Bind the media identity tail | 23 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26**, and the third tail slice in a row that needed no new routes.

The 21 `System::Object` overrides on `Album`, `Artist`, `Genre`, `Picture`, `PictureAlbum`, `Playlist`, `Song` and `MediaSource` map to the `cna_<type>_equals`, `_get_hash_code` and `_copy_name` routes each identity already carries. **`ToString` maps to the name rather than to a new route because that is literally what these overrides return** — every one of the seven bodies is `return name_;`, read rather than assumed — so a `_copy_to_string` route would be a second spelling of `_copy_name` that could later drift from it. `MediaSource` has only `ToString`, matching the canonical type, which declares no equality.

`VideoPlayer::GetFrameGenerationEXT` and `GetFramePresentationTimeEXT` map to `cna_video_player_get_frame_ext`, whose `CNA_VideoFrameEXT` already carries `generation` and `presentation_time` beside the texture. One descriptor rather than two accessors is the point of that route: a caller reading the generation and the texture through separate calls could be interrupted between them and pair a generation with the wrong frame. |
| CBIND-083 | Bind the runtime, input and content tail | 5 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26**, and the first tail slice that needed real routes.

`DrawableGameComponent`'s two rows are the `override` declarations the merge added; the operations they override were already bound, and an override is the same operation seen through the derived type — `cna_drawable_game_component_get_draw_order` and `_get_visible`.

**`TouchPanel`'s mouse-touch emulation needed two routes and got them.** `cna_touch_panel_get_mouse_touch_emulation_enabled_ext` and `_set_...` take the active game the way every other `TouchPanel` static does, and the setter validates its boolean so a non-canonical byte is refused rather than read as true. The test asserts the **XNA-matching default of off before toggling anything**, which is the assertion that would catch a build quietly flipping it, and restores it afterwards so the rest of the suite sees the panel it expects.

**`ContentManager::ResolveExistingAssetPath` is `not-applicable`, and finding out why was the work.** `ContentManager` declares `private:` and then uses a property macro, after which Doxygen reports the following members as public — so a private loader helper reached the inventory looking like API. What a caller can actually ask, the path an asset name builds to, is already `cna_content_manager_get_asset_path_size`/`_copy_asset_path`.

**A disagreement this surfaced and did not fix:** its neighbour `BuildAssetPath` sits in the same private block and is recorded **implemented** against `cna_content_manager_copy_asset_path`. That mapping is not false — the route really does call `BuildAssetPath` — but the two rows now disagree about whether a private member is bindable. Recorded in the limitations theme rather than silently changed, because that row belongs to another task.

Exports 2,942 → 2,944. 96/96 and all seven gates green. **With this row closed, `CBIND-037` has no inventory row left**, which was its remaining closure condition. |
| CBIND-084 | Bind the engine-layer foundations, resources and diagnostics | 140 | ✅ | The layer's substrate, and the slice every other CNAEXT slice depends on. **Decomposed into `CBIND-084A`–`CBIND-084C` below; the parent closes only when all three rows and every `CBIND-084` inventory row are closed.** It grew from 121 rows to 140 when `CBIND-084A` discovered that `ComputeShader::bindImage` and `::barrier` cannot be bound without the `CNA::GraphicsImageAccess` and `CNA::GraphicsMemoryBarrier` identities, which the first draft had parked in `CBIND-092`. An identity belongs with the first route that needs it, so the 19 rows moved here and `CBIND-092` dropped from 157 to 138.

**Read this before closing the row on its own rule.** As of 2026-08-27 the inventory has **zero** rows owned by `CBIND-084`, which the closure sentence above would seem to satisfy. It does not: the row that would have closed this parent — `PostProcessContext::settings`, `CBIND-084C`'s deferral — passed through `CBIND-088` and now sits with **`CBIND-100`**, because binding it means growing a published struct whose validator tests `struct_size` for exact equality. The work is unfinished; only its owner changed. **Closed 2026-08-27 by `CBIND-100`**, which made the published structure growable and appended the field. The count cell above is the original sizing rather than a live row count — the two only coincide for a row nothing has moved out of. |
| CBIND-084A | Establish the engine layer's ABI shape, and bind its version, identities, exception and compute resources | 59 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26.** `CNA/C/engine_layer.h` and `CnaCApiEngineLayer.cpp` add 25 routes over two new handle kinds (`StorageBuffer` = 125, `ComputeShader` = 126). What this slice settles, and what the rest of Phase B9 inherits:

**The refusal arm is real and measured.** Every route is declared and exported in both builds; the ones needing a native object answer `CNA_RESULT_NOT_SUPPORTED` where `CNA_CNAEXT` is off, and the layer-absent build is asserted to leave the caller's outputs *untouched* — a route that helpfully zeroed them would be indistinguishable from one that succeeded and reported zero. The promise is measured rather than argued: **both trees export the same 2,897 symbols.**

**The `EngineException` firewall arm**, ahead of the `System::Exception` arms it would otherwise fall through to. Its three property accessors need no routes: `notSupported` composes subsystem, requirement and renderer into the message, so what a caller can act on is what crosses.

**The template answer, and it is not a limitation.** `StorageBufferT<T>` is flattened onto the same handle by turning the element *type* into an element *size* the buffer carries: `cna_storage_buffer_create_typed` records count and size, so the C form enforces the template's own refusals — more elements than the buffer holds, and `getData`'s whole-range read — instead of degrading to untyped bytes. `implemented`, following the `IPackedVectorT` precedent, not a recorded partial. `getRendererEXT` is the one `not-applicable` row: it returns a renderer-private C++ interface, which is the `Internal`/`Detail` argument applied to a return type.

**Verified in two trees, and the second one earned its keep.** `cmake-build-debug` (HEADLESS, `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`) and `cmake-build-cnaext` (OPENGLES3/EasyGL, `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`) are 96/96 each; all seven C API gates pass; the ABI baseline, limitations, compatibility matrix and every export-count sentence are regenerated. EasyGL is the **only** renderer advertising `ComputeShaders`, so it is the only tree where the success path runs at all — and running it immediately found a real defect the HEADLESS tree could not: the test leaked a storage buffer, and `cna_game_destroy` refused, which is the ownership contract working. That the success arm genuinely executes was then proved by breaking it on purpose and watching the exit code move. A HEADLESS build with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` was also run and passes through the compute-absent arm, where `StorageBuffer`'s constructor refuses because the renderer has no compute.

**One defect found outside the slice and fixed here:** `cna_c_api_handle_registry_test`, `_boundary_detail_test` and `_utf8_oracle_test` compile `CnaCApiDetail.hpp` and therefore the new firewall arm, but linked no target carrying the engine layer's include path. They now link `cna_graphics_ext`, which also stops them testing a narrower firewall than production has. |
| CBIND-084B | Bind the timers, pools, factories and render-target scopes | 30 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26.** Twenty-six routes over four new owned handle kinds (`GpuTimer` = 127, `RenderTargetPool` = 128, `ShaderEffectFactory` = 129, `ScopedRenderTarget` = 130) close all 30 rows.

**The lifetime answer is counted borrows, not owner retention alone.** `RenderTargetPool` and `ShaderEffectFactory` both own their elements through `unique_ptr`, so keeping the owner alive does not stop `reset()` or `clear()` from deleting an element. A pooled target crosses as an ordinary borrowed RenderTarget2D `CNA_Handle`, and a cached shader as an ordinary borrowed `CNA_EffectHandle`, so all existing target/effect routes accept them; their C views retain the adapter owner *and* increment its active-borrow count. Reset, clear and owner destruction answer `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE` until every view is released. Releasing the view never disposes the native object, and both generic and typed dispose routes refuse it. The effect borrow shares the existing effect-child lifetime, so releasing the top-level effect handle does not unblock the factory while a parameter, technique or pass view survives.

**The RAII guard becomes a checked begin/end protocol.** Each device has a stack of opaque scope tokens. Begin retains its destination and every previous C-visible binding, constructs the canonical `ScopedRenderTarget`, and updates the C binding view; end succeeds only for the stack top. An out-of-order end is transactional — neither handle nor binding changes — and a valid end destroys the guard, restores what the native guard recorded (or the backbuffer when it could not record), updates the C view and releases the retained targets.

`EngineLayerSmoke.c` exercises every route in both builds: the `OFF` tree proves the unchanged-output refusal arms; OPENGLES3/EasyGL proves pool reuse/bytes, both borrow refusal families, an effect child prolonging the factory borrow, timer state, nested scope restoration and the out-of-order LIFO refusal. The GL-family instance now uses SDL's offscreen driver, which supplies a real Mesa GLES context without depending on the reviewer's ambient X11/Wayland session. Both libraries export the same **2,923** names. |
| CBIND-084C | Bind the pass machinery and the PBR material binding | 50 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26.** 19 routes over two more handle kinds (`FullscreenPass` = 131, `PostProcessPass` = 132), closing the last of `CBIND-084` but one.

**The abstract contract crosses as operations, not as a type.** C cannot derive from `PostProcessPass`, so one handle kind carries every concrete pass and the concrete constructors produce it. That is what makes `cna_post_process_effect_pass_get_effect` refuse a `BlitPass` **by argument** rather than by handle kind — both are the same kind, and the test asserts exactly that arm.

**The owning constructor keeps its promise without its mechanism.** A C handle's object lives in the registry as a `shared_ptr` and cannot become a `unique_ptr`, so `_create_owning` reproduces the *observable* contract: it consumes the caller's handle — proved by asserting the handle no longer destroys — retains the effect, and releases it with the pass. Deliberate deviation, recorded in the source and in the coverage row. The consumption happens only after every failable step has succeeded, so a refused call never strands the effect.

**Two naming collisions the compiler found, both real.** XNA already owns `cna_effect_pass_*` for `Microsoft::Xna::Framework::Graphics::EffectPass`, so the engine layer's pass routes carry `post_process_`. And `PbrMaterial`'s boolean getters are `isX`, not `getX` — the CNAEXT naming rule, not the XNA one.

**One row is deliberately not here: `PostProcessContext::settings`.** It points at a `RenderPipelineSettings`, and the C form of that type is still a 10-field subset of the 79-field canonical one; carrying the subset would silently apply engine defaults for everything it omits, which is a partial nobody approved rather than a binding. The field is reassigned to `CBIND-088`, which owns the settings type, by a named per-symbol override in the generator. Until then a pass applied from C sees no settings and uses its own defaults — which is exactly what a null `settings` means canonically.

**Verified in both trees**, 96/96 each, all seven gates green, ABI baseline / limitations / compatibility matrix / export-count sentences regenerated: **2,942 exports, the same number with `CNA_CNAEXT` on and off.** Both new success arms were proved to execute by breaking each on purpose and watching the exit code move to its own stage (12 and 13). Unlike `CBIND-084A`, these arms need no compute, so they run wherever the layer is present. The `detail` question the row asked was measured rather than assumed: `requireCapability`, `nameOfCapability` and `reportShaderCompileFailure` are `not-applicable` under the theme that already covers `CNA::Internal` entry points in public headers, now extended to name `CNA::Graphics::detail` too. `PostProcessPass`'s protected constructor is `not-applicable` on the `SoundEffectInstance` precedent. |
| CBIND-085 | Bind the lights and the shadow subsystem | 209 | ✅ | The largest slice in the phase. **Decomposed into `CBIND-085A`–`CBIND-085C` below; the parent closes only when all three rows and every `CBIND-085` inventory row are closed.** The split follows the dependency rather than the file list: the value types first, because the shadow maps and the prepass both produce them. **Closed 2026-08-27.** All four children are ✅ and the slice's inventory rows are all closed. The one row that is not here went to `CBIND-086` rather than staying open: `ClusteredShadowPolicyEXT::select` takes a `ClusteredLightSetEXT`, so it waits for the slice that binds the type, recorded as a named per-symbol override in the generator rather than as a comment. The 209 rows became four commits and 148 routes; the decomposition into A/B1/B2/C1/C2 was worth it, and the rule it suggests for the rest of the phase is **one commit per ~40 routes**. |
| CBIND-085A | Bind the light value types | 44 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26.** `CNA_DirectionalLightEXT`, `CNA_PointLightEXT`, `CNA_SpotLightEXT`, `CNA_PunctualLightEXT` with `CNA_PunctualLightKindEXT`, and `CNA_ShadowCascadeStateEXT` — five fixed-layout structs and five `_init` routes.

**All five work in both builds**, which is new for this phase: filling a light with its defaults needs no engine-layer object, so the routes are unconditional and the test asserts them unconditionally too. That is what would fail if someone later guarded them by reflex. `PunctualLightEXT` and `ShadowCascadeStateEXT` are not behind `CNA_CNAEXT` at all — they live in `modules/graphics` — and the other three have their defaults checked against the canonical aggregate wherever the layer is compiled in.

**The slice found a defect in `CBIND-084C` and fixed it.** Writing these defaults meant asking what a value-initialized `Matrix` actually is, and the answer is **all zeros** — `Matrix::Matrix()` sets all sixteen fields to 0, and `cna_matrix_init` documents itself as zero-filled. `cna_post_process_context_init` had been written to fill its four matrices with the *identity*, because that reads like the friendlier default. It is the wrong one: a pass reading a defaulted context from C would have seen a different projection from a C++ caller reading a defaulted `PostProcessContext`. Corrected here, in the same commit that would have repeated it, and the test now asserts zeros in both places.

`kMaxCascades` becomes `CNA_SHADOW_CASCADE_MAX_EXT` with a `static_assert` tying them together, so growing the canonical array cannot silently leave the C form behind. The shadow textures default to `CNA_INVALID_HANDLE`, which is a handle's spelling of the canonical null pointer. Exports 2,944 → 2,949; 96/96 in both trees, all eight gates green, and the light arm proved to execute by breaking it. |
| CBIND-085B1 | Bind the two 2D shadow maps | 39 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** `ShadowMap` and `SpotShadowMap` as owned handles with 37 routes, plus one row moved here from `CBIND-093`.

**Casting and sampling are different questions, and the slice binds both.** A map's `_is_supported` answers whether the shadow can be *cast* — the canonical flag is the caster effect that actually exists and links, not a capability promise. Whether anything can *sample* the result is `GraphicsDevice::SupportsShadowSamplingEXT`, which was planned under `CBIND-093` and is **moved here**, on the same reasoning that moved the identities into `CBIND-084A`: it belongs with the first route that needs it. A caller with only the first would ship a shadow texture nothing reads, which looks exactly like a scene with no occluders.

The caster effects and the shadow texture are **borrows** that keep the map alive, and destroying a map is refused while one is outstanding. That guard was documented in the header before it was implemented, and the test caught the gap — the assertion that destroy is refused failed, which is the doc and the code disagreeing out loud.

**Two defects in the test, both worth recording because the second crashed.** `cna_shadow_map_create` clears its output before validating, so passing a live handle to the refusal case stranded the map that had just been made. Then `applySkinnedCaster` turned out to validate its palette **before** it checks `supported_`, so an empty palette is refused on every renderer — the test expected success, the refusal threw with the pass still open, and a map destroyed in that state disposes a target the device still has bound: `terminate`, not a test failure. Every exit from that block now goes through one cleanup, which is the shape a test touching an object with an open scope has to have.

`CApiRouteTestCoverage` then caught `cna_shadow_map_get_skinned_caster_effect` as a route no test named. Covered rather than budgeted — a matrix row saying *implemented* is not evidence that anything calls the route, which is exactly what that gate exists to say. Exports 2,949 → 2,986; 96/96 in both trees, all eight gates green, arm proved to execute. |
| CBIND-085B2 | Bind the cascaded and cube shadow maps | 44 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 40 routes over two more handle kinds, closing every `CBIND-085B` row except `applyToReceiver`, which waits for `CBIND-085C`'s receiver contract.

**Two result shapes the 2D maps did not need.** `computeSplitDistances` returns a vector, so it takes the campaign's count/copy form — asking with no room reports the count and writes nothing, which the test asserts by leaving a sentinel in the destination. `computeFrustumCorners` returns a fixed `std::array<Vector3, 8>`, so its C form takes a destination of exactly `CNA_FRUSTUM_CORNER_COUNT_EXT` rather than a capacity a caller could get wrong, with a `static_assert` tying the two counts together. `computeBoundingSphere` returns a radius *and* fills a centre, which becomes two outputs.

**Deviation, deliberate:** the canonical constructor **clamps** a cascade count the atlas cannot hold; the C route **refuses** it. A C caller who asked for five cascades and silently got four would have no way to notice, and the count is the one argument that changes what every later call means.

**The cube texture needed its release path checked, not assumed.** `cna_texturecube_destroy` releases the handle **without** disposing the object — verified by reading it — which is what makes handing out an aliasing view safe. Disposing a map's own cube from underneath it is exactly the crash `CBIND-085B1` hit, so this was checked before the borrow was written rather than after.

**`CApiBoolContractSmoke` caught a real ordering defect.** `cna_cascaded_shadow_map_set_debug_tint_enabled` validated its boolean *inside* the resource lookup, so with an invalid handle it answered a handle error — which the gate reads as "accepted the byte". `CBIND-067`'s discipline is that the bool is refused **before** the handle is resolved, so the answer is the same whatever handle came with it. It passed in the `OFF` tree, where the route refuses for a different reason entirely; only the `ON` tree could show it. Exports 2,986 → 3,026; 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green, arm proved to execute. |
| CBIND-085C1 | Bind the shadow-receiver contract and the clustered shadow budget | 31 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 27 routes, and the row `CBIND-085B2` had to leave behind.

**`IShadowReceiverEXT` gets the `PostProcessPass` answer: the operations cross, the type does not.** C can neither implement the interface nor hold one, so the fourteen operations become `cna_effect_*_ext` routes applied to an effect handle, each asking the **concrete** effect by `dynamic_cast` whether it is a receiver. That is the honest test — `BasicEffect` implements it, a sprite effect does not, and the answer belongs to the object rather than to a flag this ABI could get out of step with. A non-receiver is refused by **argument**, not by handle: the handle is perfectly valid, it is the type that cannot answer.

**One lifetime decision worth its comment.** `getShadowMapEXT` returns a fresh name for the same texture, because the canonical interface stores a raw pointer and has no handle to give back. It deliberately does **not** count a borrow the way the shadow maps do: the effect *lends* the shadow map rather than owning it, so a count would refuse to destroy the effect for a reason that protects nothing, while implying this ABI keeps the texture alive when it does not.

**`applyToReceiver`, deferred by `CBIND-085B2`, closes here** — and its canonical precondition survives: applying before `update()` is refused, because there are no cascade matrices to give and a defaulted state would silently shadow nothing.

**Two more canonical preconditions the test expected wrongly and now asserts.** `getScore` refuses an index the last selection did not score rather than answering zero, which a caller would read as "scored, and worthless". And `applyToReceiver` throws before `update()`. Both were found by the test failing, which is the third slice running where reading the canonical body first would have been quicker than guessing.

`ClusteredShadowPolicyEXT` is the phase's first **pure CPU** object: no device, every route usable wherever the layer is, still parented to a game so its lifetime is accounted for. `select` is not here — it takes a `ClusteredLightSetEXT`, so that one row waits for `CBIND-086`. Exports 3,026 → 3,053; 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green, both arms proved to execute. |
| CBIND-085C2 | Bind the depth/normal prepass and contact shadows | 51 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 44 routes, and **`CBIND-085` now owns no planned row.**

**The slice that passed first time, because the preconditions were read rather than guessed.** The previous three slices each lost a build-and-bisect cycle to a contract discovered by a test failing; this one began by grepping every `throw` in both canonical bodies. All of them survive as assertions: a non-positive size is refused rather than clamped, `begin` refuses a near plane that is not positive or a far plane not beyond it — depth is normalised by the far plane — and `resize` and the velocity switch are refused **while a pass is open**, rather than reallocating the target the device is drawing into.

**Two shapes, both already settled elsewhere in the phase.** The GLSL-returning statics take the string copy-out form, and the test asserts the packed and unpacked decoders **differ** — a single shared string would be the plausible bug. `packDepth` writes four floats through references, which becomes four outputs, and pack/unpack is asserted to round-trip; the canonical clamp one texel short of 1.0 is asserted too, since `fract(1.0)` is 0 and an unclamped far-plane depth would read back as the *nearest* possible surface.

**Where a canonical setter clamps, the C route clamps.** `setRoughness` corrects an out-of-range value rather than refusing it, and `ContactShadowPass`'s setters store what they are given — a step count of zero traces nothing, which is a way of switching the pass off rather than an error. Refusing either would be this ABI inventing a rule the layer does not have.

`ContactShadowPass` is a `PostProcessPass`, so it adds **no handle kind**: it is created here and driven by the shared operations, with its own settings discriminating on the concrete type. `isOccluded` keeps its strict band — a sample exactly at the bias is not occluded, which is the arm a `>=` would get wrong — and `combineVisibility` clamps both inputs before multiplying, so a caller cannot brighten a pixel by passing a visibility above one. Exports 3,053 → 3,097; 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green, arm proved to execute. |
| CBIND-086 | Bind clustered forward lighting | 117 → 112 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 by `CBIND-087A`, not by a tidy-up.** All three child rows closed on the 27th, leaving one inventory row — `ClusteredForwardEffect::contribution(…, const PbrMaterialExtensions&)` — which could not be bound until `PbrMaterialExtensions` existed in C. `CBIND-087A` bound that type and the overload with it, so this parent went from 🟨 to ✅ in the commit that removed its blocker rather than in one that lowered the bar. Five further rows left this slice for `CBIND-091` (3, still open) and `CBIND-087` (2, closed by `CBIND-087A`) under named overrides, which is why the row count reads 112 rather than 117. The split follows the dependency: the value types and the set before the grid, assignment and buffer that consume them, and the effect and the compute path last.

**A correction this row carried, and the reason to read a claim before repeating it.** This row used to say `ClusteredLightCompute` "needs `SupportsComputeShadersEXT()` on top of the capability", and `CBIND-085`'s rows said the same about their own subsystems. That sentence came from `CLAUDE.md`, which names four `GraphicsDevice` queries; **only three of them are `GraphicsDevice` methods.** `SupportsComputeShadersEXT` is declared on `CNA::Internal::Renderers::IGraphicsRenderer`, an internal interface the inventory excludes by path — it has **no row anywhere**, in `CBIND-093` or elsewhere, so there was nothing to bind and nothing to move. What a C caller asks instead already exists: `cna_graphics_device_supports_capability` with `CNA_GRAPHICS_CAPABILITY_COMPUTE_SHADERS`, and `GraphicsCapability.hpp` states outright that this capability **is** the one answered by `IGraphicsRenderer::SupportsComputeShadersEXT()` rather than by a renderer's own switch. The capability is not a weaker question here; it is the same question. |
| CBIND-086A | Bind the clustered light values and the light set | 29 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 16 routes; exports 3,097 → 3,113. Passed on the first run, the second slice in a row to do so since the grep-first practice above became the rule.

**The set is a collection of values, and that settles the lifetime question rather than deferring it.** `getAt` returns a reference and `getLights` a vector reference, but the element is a POD: the C form copies out, hands out no view onto anything the set owns, needs no counted borrow, and is never refused destruction — the opposite of `CBIND-084B`'s pool, decided by reading the element type rather than by copying the nearest precedent.

**The two refusals are kept apart because a caller acts on them differently.** The canonical code throws `length_error` for a full set and `invalid_argument` for an unusable light; the firewall would flatten both. A full set is `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE` — drop a light — and the bound exists because the uploaded buffer and the shader's index width are sized from it, so it is refused rather than grown. An unusable light is `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_ARGUMENT` — fix the light just built. `isUsable` is bound so a caller can ask before being refused, and the test asserts the two agree.

**Three arms measured, and the third found something that is not in this slice.** Arm 1 (`cmake-build-debug`, `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`) and arm 2 (`cmake-build-cnaext`, OPENGLES3/EasyGL) both pass, with the arm proved to execute by breaking it. Arm 3 — the same tree reconfigured to HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` — **fails, and fails identically with this slice's test removed**, so it is pre-existing. See `CBIND-097`. |

Preconditions already read out of the body: `add` refuses past `kMaxLights` (256) rather than growing, **because the uploaded buffer and the shader's index width are sized from that bound**; `add` and `replaceAt` refuse a light `isUsable` rejects; and `replaceAt`, `removeAt`, `getAt` and `getBoundsAt` refuse an index the set does not hold. Each becomes an assertion. |
| CBIND-086B | Bind the cluster grid, the light assignment and the upload buffer | 46 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 36 routes; exports 3,113 → 3,149. Passed on the first run — the third consecutive slice to do so under the grep-first practice.

**Every site was classified, not copied.** A grid dimension outside its range is a **contract** and is refused, because the cluster count is what the light-index list is sized from and a quietly smaller grid would size a list the caller did not mean. `sliceForViewDistance` is a **correction** and stays clamped, because a point outside the frustum belongs to the nearest slice — which is what a renderer wants when a light straddles the edge.

**One off-by-one worth the reading.** `sliceDistance` accepts the slice count *inclusively*: there is one more boundary than slice, and the last names the far edge. The obvious `>=` bound would have silently lost the far plane, and nothing else in the suite would have noticed — so it is asserted from both sides.

**The buffer's lifetime question dissolved on reading, the way the light set's did.** It owns three textures and **lends none of them** — the canonical class keeps them private and exposes only `bind` — so there is no borrow to count and destruction is never refused. The pool's pattern would have been the wrong one to copy, for the second time in this phase.

`adopt`'s four refusals are kept apart rather than flattened, because each says something different about what the caller got wrong; `lightsInCluster` returns a span into the assignment's own storage and the C form copies, so a recompute cannot dangle it; and `upload` refuses a mismatched trio, because an inconsistent one would light the wrong objects with the wrong lamps rather than fail visibly. |
| CBIND-086C | Bind the clustered forward effect and the compute path | 42 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 32 routes; exports 3,149 → 3,181, the same number with `CNA_CNAEXT` on and off and **zero symbols differing** when the two export lists are compared name by name. 36 of the 42 rows closed; the other six are deferred with named owners, below. `ClusteredShadowPolicyEXT::select`'s `SYMBOL_OWNER_OVERRIDES` entry was deleted in this commit, and **the `CBIND-079` gate immediately proved why that matters**: with the override gone the row fell back to the slice table, which owns it to `CBIND-085` — a task recorded ✅ — and the coverage generator refused to run until the `clustered-shadow-policy` rule was widened to cover the member that had just been implemented. The defect the gate exists to catch was reproduced by the fix for it.

**The compute path degrades rather than refuses, and only reading the body says so.** `ClusteredLightCompute::assign` falls back to the CPU `ClusteredLightAssignment::assign` when no compute program compiled, so on a renderer without compute shaders it still produces a correct assignment — the class named after a GPU feature is not gated on that feature. This reframed the third arm before a line was written: arm 3 is a *fallback* arm, not a refusal arm. The fallback also happens **before** the canonical validations, so both paths would otherwise answer the same mistake with two differently-worded refusals; the C form checks the grid's projection up front so one message is given either way.

**The test is a cross-path oracle rather than a capability assertion.** Stage 18 sorts the same three lights through `ClusteredLightAssignment` and through `ClusteredLightCompute` and compares the offsets and the indices element by element, so the GPU and the CPU are held to one answer on every renderer; `used_compute` is then asserted equal to `is_supported`, which is what fails if the compute arm ever stops executing where it exists. `getUnsupportedReason` is empty exactly when the program compiled, asserted as one equivalence rather than two conditions.

**Three arms, three measurements.** Arm 1 (`cmake-build-debug`, `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`): the layer is absent and every route refuses. Arm 2 (`cmake-build-cnaext`, OPENGLES3/EasyGL): compute compiles and the GPU path runs — **proved by asserting `used_compute` is false and watching the exit code move to 21, stage 18's own code**. Arm 3 (`build-probe`, HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`): compute is absent, the fallback runs, and stages 18 and 19 pass. Arm 3 stopped at stage 11 for the pre-existing `CBIND-097` defect when this slice landed, so it was measured by short-circuiting stages 11–17 and reading past it. `CBIND-097` closed later the same day, and arm 3 now passes end to end without that workaround.

**Six rows deferred with named owners, and one of them could not use the override mechanism.** `setMaterialExtensions`/`getMaterialExtensions` → `CBIND-087`; `setAreaLight`/`setLightProbe`/`setLightProbeVolume` → `CBIND-091`. Each takes a type another slice owns; the effect itself is bound and only the argument is missing, so their `clear*`/`has*` partners are bound here — neither names the foreign type, and `hasAreaLight` reporting a steady false is honest rather than useless. The sixth is `contribution`'s `PbrMaterialExtensions` overload, which **shares a qualified name with the fifteen-scalar overload this slice implements**; `SYMBOL_OWNER_OVERRIDES` is keyed by qualified name and would have moved both, and a rule cannot carry a `planned` status (the generator accepts only `implemented`/`partial`/`not-applicable`). It therefore stays owned by `CBIND-086`, discriminated by `signature_regex`, and is the single row keeping this parent open.

**Two leaks the first regex draft would have shipped, both caught by reading the approved set rather than the row count.** A rule keyed only on the class name swallowed four of the five deferred members and marked them ✅ implemented — routes that do not exist — and it also swallowed the deleted copy constructor, overriding the `not-applicable` the generator gives a deleted operation. `approved_symbols` is computed and then *inspected symbol by symbol*, not trusted because the total looked plausible.

**Clamps versus contracts, classified per site as the standing practice requires.** Contracts, refused: `begin` against a buffer that holds nothing has no cluster table for the shader to walk; a transmissive material with no opaque frame is an opaque object where a glass one was asked for, which the canonical code calls "refused rather than approximated"; a non-positive stride sizes the light-index list. Corrections, clamped: base colour and metallic to zero-to-one, ambient floored at zero because a negative ambient would subtract light never added, and **roughness to 0.04, not zero**, because a perfectly smooth surface collapses the specular lobe to a point the shader cannot integrate. The test asserts 0.04 rather than 0 — a floor asserted at the wrong value is a clamp that can stop clamping unnoticed.

96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green, ABI baseline / `limitations.json` / compatibility matrix / release gate / export-count sentences regenerated. Two corrections to the artifacts were needed and are worth naming: `docs/c-api/LIMITATIONS.md` is **generated** from `tools/c-api/limitations.json`, so editing the document directly left the gate failing until the source was fixed; and the plan's own snapshot sentence still carried `2,942` as the on/off-equal figure, stale since `CBIND-084C` and never updated by the three slices after it. It now states the measured 3,181 and how it was measured. **`CApi_SensorDeviceSmoke` failed once in a batch run and passed on two full reruns and in isolation** — recorded as a second unattributed occurrence of the flake class `CApi_MediaPlayerSmoke` already showed, in a different suite, and not changed without a reproduction. |
| CBIND-087 | Bind PBR materials and transparency | 179 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 at zero planned rows.** All four child rows closed and the inventory has none left under this task; nine rows moved to `CBIND-091` under named overrides along the way. **Decomposed into `CBIND-087A`–`CBIND-087D` below; the parent closed only when all four rows and every `CBIND-087` inventory row were closed** — the same rule `CBIND-084`, `CBIND-085` and `CBIND-086` use. The split follows the dependency and, secondarily, the contract density: the extension value type before the material that carries it, the material before the effects that consume it, and the glTF bridge and the transparency pipeline last, because they produce and draw what the first three define. **`CBIND-086C` moved two rows here** — `ClusteredForwardEffect::setMaterialExtensions` and `getMaterialExtensions`, which name `PbrMaterialExtensions`; a third, `contribution`'s extensions overload, could not be moved because it shares a qualified name with an implemented overload and stays under `CBIND-086` until this slice lands. `PbrMaterial`, `PbrMaterialExtensions`, `ThinFilmIridescence`, `GltfMaterialBridge`, `TransparencyMode`, `TransparentDrawList` and `WeightedBlendedTransparency`, plus `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect` from `modules/graphics`. Materials carry texture references, so the borrowed-lifetime rules the campaign already settled for effects apply unchanged — a material never owns the `Texture2D` a caller hands it. |
| CBIND-087A | Bind the PBR material extensions and thin-film iridescence | 64 | ✅ | `PbrMaterialExtensions` (59), `ThinFilmIridescence` (3) and the two `ClusteredForwardEffect` rows `CBIND-086C` moved here. **This slice also closes `CBIND-086`**: its one remaining row is `contribution`'s `PbrMaterialExtensions` overload, which becomes bindable the moment this type exists, and binding it here retires the parent rather than leaving it open across four more slices.

Read before writing, as the standing practice requires: **`PbrMaterialExtensions` throws nowhere.** All twenty-three setters are **corrections**, and the interesting part is which ones are *not* corrected — `clearcoatNormalScale`, `thicknessFactor`, `attenuationDistance`, `iridescenceIor` and the two iridescence thickness bounds take any value, while the other nine clamp to zero-to-one. That asymmetry is the thing a test has to pin, because a clamp added to a currently-free field would never be noticed otherwise.

The type is a **value with nine borrowed `Texture2D*` members**, so it is bound as an owned handle rather than a C struct: a plain struct would put raw texture pointers in caller-writable memory. The borrowed-lifetime rule this row already recorded holds — the extensions never own a texture handed to them. `ThinFilmIridescence` is two static pure functions and needs no handle at all.

**Done 2026-08-27.** 64 routes; exports 3,181 → 3,245, the same number with `CNA_CNAEXT` on and off and zero symbols differing when the two lists are compared name by name. **This slice also closed `CBIND-086`**, whose last row was the `contribution` overload that needed this type.

**The pre-slice grep was wrong, and finding out how is the most useful thing in this row.** It reported "no throws, everything clamps", and the second half of that was false: `PbrMaterialExtensions` corrects in **three** shapes, not one. Nine members clamp with `std::clamp`. Five use a **guarded assignment** — `if (value >= bound) field = value;` — which leaves the previous value in place, so an out-of-range write is a *silent no-op* rather than a value pinned to the bound; four of those guard at zero and `iridescenceIor` guards at **one**, because an index of refraction below one describes a medium light speeds up in. One, `attenuationDistance`, floors at zero with a bare ternary, writing the bound where its four neighbours keep the old value.

**Why the practice missed it, and what to do about it.** The standing rule says to grep every `throw` and every `clamp`. A guarded assignment contains neither word — it looks like ordinary control flow — so the grep returned a clean sheet on six setters that all correct. **Amend the practice: the grep opens the reading, it does not conclude it.** A setter is only classified once its body has been read or its behaviour round-tripped, and the cheap way to do the latter is what caught this here — write every scalar out of range at both ends and read it back. That test distinguishes all three shapes from each other; a test that only checked the six known clamps would have passed while six routes documented behaviour they do not have.

The header carried the wrong claim for a few minutes too, saying these fields were "stored as given". Both the documentation and the test now state which shape each field uses and why the bound is where it is.

**Three arms.** Layer off: every route refuses. EasyGL: passes, and stage 20 is proved to execute by asserting a guarded setter clamps to zero and watching the exit code move to 23. HEADLESS with the layer on: passes; this slice touches no renderer capability, so all three arms agree. Arm 3 was read past the `CBIND-097` stall at stage 11, which closed later the same day — from here on the third arm needs no workaround.

**Two `SYMBOL_OWNER_OVERRIDES` entries expired here and were deleted in the same commit**, the discipline `CBIND-086C` established: `setMaterialExtensions` and `getMaterialExtensions` no longer wait for anything. The `CBIND-086C` forward-effect rule was widened to match, leaving only the two `CBIND-091` deferrals behind.

**`CApiRouteTestCoverage` caught a real gap before the commit did.** The first draft exercised one texture slot of nine and the gate named the other sixteen routes outright — nine near-identical get/set pairs are exactly where a copy-paste error binds the wrong member, and only calling each one can tell. The test now covers all nine, and asserts that binding one slot leaves the next one empty, which is the assertion that would fail if two getters read the same member.

96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green, every artifact regenerated. |
| CBIND-087B | Bind the PBR material and its transparency mode | 45 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** Three routes, not forty-five: exports 3,245 → 3,248. Re-grepping rather than inheriting `CBIND-087A`'s finding was right, and it found the opposite — **`PbrMaterial` corrects nothing at all.** Every canonical setter is a bare assignment, which no idiom grep can establish, only reading the bodies.

**Most of this slice was already built and nobody had said so.** `CBIND-035F7` bound the material as two fixed-layout PODs — the frozen `CNA_PbrMaterial` and the versioned `CNA_PbrMaterialEXT`, which already carries every canonical field including the specular factors, the alpha mode and both per-slot arrays. The 41 rows were planned because no rule claimed them, not because routes were missing. What genuinely needed building is what a POD cannot express: `cna_pbr_material_ext_equals`, `_get_hash_code` and `_copy_to_string`, plus `CNA_PBR_TEXTURE_SLOT_COUNT` and the `CNA_TransparencyMode` identities, each pinned to its canonical ordinal by a `static_assert`. This is the fourth time in the phase a slice turned out to be mostly rule-writing; the row estimate measures inventory rows, not work.

**The one correction in the canonical type never reaches C, and that is a deviation rather than a gap.** `getTextureCoordinateSet` and `getTextureTransform` route their slot through an `IndexOf` that folds an out-of-range slot onto base colour. The C form carries both as fixed arrays inside the structure, so there is no slot argument to validate and no fold to reproduce — an out-of-range index is the caller's own array-bounds error, not something this ABI silently redirects. The POD mapping removes a surprise the enum mapping would have had to carry.

The three value routes are gated on the layer **together**, though the structure is describable in either build, because they answer with the canonical rule. Splitting them — reimplementing equality field by field in C while the hash stayed gated — would make equality answerable in a build where the hash consistent with it is not.

**A latent gap from `CBIND-087A` was found and closed here.** Both slices' rules were keyed on `[A-Za-z0-9_~]+`, which does not match `operator==`, so `PbrMaterialExtensions::operator==`/`!=` had stayed planned while the `_equals` route that maps them was written and tested. It did not trip the `CBIND-079` gate because the rows named the still-open parent rather than the closed child. Both regexes now match operator names, and the two extra rows close in this commit rather than being left for whoever noticed next. **A character class is a silent filter: it fails by matching less, and nothing reports it.**

Three arms, all passing; stage 21 proved to execute by inverting the hash assertion and watching the exit code move to 24. The layer-absent build asserts all three routes refuse and write nothing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-087C | Bind the PBR effects | 32 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27. No new routes and no new exports — 3,248 before and after.** The sizing note above was wrong about what these rows are: all thirty-two are `override` members of the shadow-receiver interface, which `CBIND-085B1` already bound as `cna_effect_*_ext` operations on the `Effect` handle, resolved by `dynamic_cast` and refused **by argument** when the effect does not implement it. The sixteen throws counted in the estimate are in the effects' own shader and parameter code, not in the members these rows name.

**What the slice owed was evidence, and that is not a formality.** Stage 13 proved the receiver contract against `BasicEffect` and against a `SpriteEffect` that must be refused; nothing proved these two types resolve. Both live in `modules/graphics` rather than the engine layer, so they exist in builds where the routes that drive them do not, and a route that resolved for `BasicEffect` while silently refusing a `PbrEffect` would leave every PBR shadow inert with no error a caller could see. Stage 22 runs every member against both handles and **reads each value back**, which is what separates "the cast succeeded" from "the call was accepted and dropped".

**Four rows deferred with named owners**: `set`/`getImageBasedLightEXT` on both effects take an `ImageBasedLightEXT`, which `CBIND-091` owns — that slice goes 140 → 144 rows.

**A regex that was too broad, caught by the generator rather than by review.** The first draft matched `[A-Za-z0-9_]+EXT$` on both classes, which collided with the existing `color-matrix-and-pbr-effects-complete-contract` rule on `SetOwnedSpecularMapEXT` and would also have claimed the four deferred rows as implemented. The rule now names its fourteen members explicitly. This is the third slice in a row where a regex convenience nearly marked unbound work as bound; **an alternation of real names is worth the characters.**

Three arms, all passing. Proved to execute by asserting the wrong filter radius and watching the exit code move to stage 22. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-087D | Bind the glTF material bridge and the transparency pipeline | 38 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 28 routes; exports 3,248 → 3,276, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing. **`CBIND-087` closes with it**, at zero planned rows.

**The bridge is written against a C++20 concept, not a type.** A concept has no C form and is not a callable operation, so the C bridge takes structures that name exactly the members each concept requires and builds a local type from them to satisfy the constraint. The concept stays the contract. The `_init` routines fill **glTF's own defaults** — 1.0 metallic and roughness, 1.5 IOR, 0.5 alpha cutoff — not this layer's, because an omitted glTF factor means the specification's value; the test asserts those rather than whatever the structure happened to zero-initialize to. One value is deliberately lossy and canonically so: `baseColorFactor` is four floats and an albedo is a `Color`, so it quantises to eight bits per channel — asserted with a fractional value, since 1.0 survives any quantiser.

**`WeightedBlendedTransparency`'s eight throws are two different mistakes and stay apart.** `invalid_argument` → `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `logic_error` → `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE`. A caller acts on them differently — fix the number, or fix the order of the calls — and the firewall unaided would tell them a size of zero and a bracket left open are the same error.

**A defect found and not fixed *here*: `CBIND-098`** (fixed in the engine layer on 2026-08-28; see its own row). `begin` set `accumulating_` **after** its `if (!isSupported()) return;`, so on a renderer without the resolve it opens nothing and the matching `end` refuses. That is the `CBIND-097` shape in a different class, and it is reproduced rather than corrected, per the standing rule that this campaign audits engine-layer behaviour instead of changing it. The test branches on `is_supported` so **both** paths assert; the non-opening branch is what pins the defect in place, so a later fix shows up as a test that must change.

**Two defects in the test itself, both found by the break-proof rather than by review — which is the entire argument for doing it.**

*The masked assertion.* The unsupported branch read `ok = <expr>` where every other line reads `ok = ok && <expr>`. Because its enclosing guard is `if (ok && supported == CNA_TRUE) … else …`, the else is reached both when the run is healthy and when `ok` is already false — so a plain assignment there **erased every earlier failure**. Breaking the accumulating assertion made this visible immediately: arm 2 moved to stage 23 and arm 3 kept passing. The arm that then pinned `CBIND-098` was the arm that could not fail. **A guard that does not test `ok` must never open with a bare `ok =`.** Three pre-existing sites in stages 13 and 15 share the shape and are recorded as `CBIND-099`.

*The abort on the failure path.* With the break in place, arm 2 dumped core: a failing assertion skipped the branch that closes the bracket, and destroying a resolve with accumulation still open aborts inside the canonical destructor. Every exit now runs through one cleanup, the discipline `CBIND-085B1` learned the same way.

Three arms, all passing; stage 23 proved to execute on **both** its branches, on EasyGL and on HEADLESS. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-088 | Bind the render pipeline and its settings | 118 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 at zero inventory rows.** Both child rows closed, and the one row neither could bind — `PostProcessContext::settings` — moved to `CBIND-100` with a named override, because binding it means appending a field to a published struct whose validator cannot tolerate growth. The parent closes on the campaign's own rule (no `CBIND-088` inventory row left) rather than on the row being finished in spirit; what remains is genuinely another task's, and is named as such rather than left implicit.

*o through an `AtLeast` helper that a grep for `clamp` does not find, the same blind spot `CBIND-087A` recorded. A plain POD with direct field access would lose every one of them, and a caller writing `5.0` into a field the canonical setter clamps to `0.1`, or `-1` into one it floors at zero, would hand the pass a value it never accepts from C++.

The resolution keeps the POD **and** the clamps: the structure is a *description*, and the conversion to the canonical type runs every field through the canonical setter, exactly as `CNA_PbrMaterialEXT` does. A `_normalize` route makes that observable without needing a pipeline, so a caller can ask what the engine will actually store. The canonical source states the reasoning for the asymmetry outright — the ten clamped values are the ones whose out-of-range case is a division by zero or a negative intensity, and the rest are left alone because a settings bag that clamped to one pass's limits would silently constrain another's. | `RenderPipeline` and `RenderPipelineSettings`. Two headers and 117 rows: `RenderPipelineSettings` alone is 79, almost all of it POD fields, which makes this the phase's clearest candidate for a fixed-layout struct plus an ABI-layout test rather than 79 accessor pairs. Decide that shape here and the rest of the phase gets shorter. **`CBIND-084C` added a row to this slice**: `PostProcessContext::settings` points at this type, and binding it against a subset would have applied engine defaults for every omitted field, so the field waits for the full settings binding here. |
| CBIND-088A | Bind the render pipeline settings | 79 | ✅ | `RenderPipelineSettings` — forty-six accessor pairs, a constructor, and the tonemapping/quality identities with their string parsers. Bound as a versioned `CNA_RenderPipelineSettingsEXT` POD beside the frozen ten-field `CNA_RenderPipelineSettings`, on the `CNA_PbrMaterialEXT` precedent: the existing structure's layout cannot change within an ABI major, so the full shape arrives under a new name rather than by rearranging it.

**Throws nowhere; corrects thirty-two of forty-six setters, in two shapes and six ranges.** The test must write every corrected field out of range and read back the *specific* bound — a single shared `0..1` expectation would pass while four fields clamped to the wrong limit — and must write each of the fourteen genuinely free fields out of range and require the value to survive, which is what fails if a correction is ever added where the canonical code has none. The `AtLeast` floors are the ones a `clamp` grep misses; they were found by reading, per the practice `CBIND-087A` amended.

**Done 2026-08-27.** Four routes for seventy-nine rows; exports 3,276 → 3,280, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing.

**The measured classification, which is what the whole slice turns on:** ten setters clamp to a two-sided range across four different upper bounds (`0.5`, `1.0`, `0.25`, `0.1`), twenty-one floor at a minimum (nineteen at zero, one at `kMinimumGamma`, one at `kMinimumFxaaEdgeThreshold`), and sixteen correct nothing. The structure is therefore a **description**, not the stored state: every conversion runs each field through its canonical setter, and `_normalize` performs that round trip in place so a caller can ask what the engine will actually store.

**The test asserts thirty-one separate bounds, not one shared range — and that was proved to matter.** Replacing the `0..0.1` expectation for chromatic aberration with the common `0..1` moves the exit code to stage 24. A single shared expectation would have shipped four fields clamped to the wrong limit with a green suite. Each floor is additionally required to leave a *large* value untouched, which is what separates a floor from a clamp; without that half, twenty-one fields could quietly gain an upper bound unnoticed.

**Twenty-two rows stayed with `CBIND-035F7`** — the class, its constructor, and the ten fields the frozen structure already carries. They are bound. But it is worth recording what that binding does not do: **direct field access on the frozen `CNA_RenderPipelineSettings` does not apply the corrections**, so a caller writing `gamma = 0` there holds a value the canonical setter would have floored to `0.01`. The `EXT` structure carries the same ten fields and normalizes them; new code should take that route. This is not a defect introduced here and not one this slice can fix — the frozen structure's behaviour is fixed with its layout — but it is the kind of thing a coverage row reading "✅ implemented" hides, so it is written down rather than left to be rediscovered.

**Two mistakes of mine, both caught by machinery rather than by reading.** The rule's first draft matched the whole class and collided with `CBIND-035F7`'s; the generator refused rather than silently re-attributing twenty-two closed rows — the third time in the phase a broad regex nearly claimed another slice's work. And the serialized-text test used a space separator where the canonical parser splits on `;`, which the run reported as zero fields applied; the format was then read from the parser rather than assumed.

Three arms, all passing; the layer-absent build asserts all four routes refuse and write nothing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-088B | Bind the render pipeline | 39 | ✅ | `RenderPipeline` (38) and `PostProcessContext::settings` (1), which `CBIND-084C` deferred here because binding it against a subset would have applied engine defaults for every omitted field.

**Seven throws, two kinds, and they must not arrive as one result** — the `CBIND-087D` rule: `invalid_argument` for a non-positive size or an inverted near/far pair, `logic_error` for a frame already open, a frame not open, and a device-resource release attempted mid-frame. `begin` additionally refuses when the pipeline has never been sized, which is a third distinct state worth its own message rather than being folded into "no frame is open".

Check `begin`/`end` for the `CBIND-097`/`CBIND-098` shape before writing the test: if `frameOpen_` is set only on a supported path, this slice inherits that defect too and must pin it rather than assert success. (Both of those classes have since been corrected in the engine layer; the check is still the right one to make.)

**Done 2026-08-27.** 30 routes; exports 3,280 → 3,308. 36 of the 39 rows closed.

**The check this row demanded came back clean, and only because it was made.** `RenderPipeline::begin` marks the frame open *before* any support check and `end` clears it *before* any early return, so the bracket is symmetric on every renderer — unlike `WeightedBlendedTransparency` (`CBIND-098`) and the shadow maps (`CBIND-097`) **as they stood then**; both were corrected later, which is what makes this row's negative finding a finding rather than a coincidence. Both halves of the test therefore assert success rather than branching on support: three classes in this phase had the same surface shape and only two of them had the defect.

**One pair of throws the exception type cannot separate, and the binding does the separating.** Of the six throws, `invalid_argument` maps to `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_ARGUMENT` and `logic_error` to `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE` — but `begin` throws `logic_error` for **two different states**: a frame already open, and a pipeline that has never been sized. The pipeline exposes no width accessor, so nothing after the fact can tell them apart. The C resource therefore remembers whether `resize` ever succeeded and answers the two with their own messages. The test asserts both, and proving it executes used exactly that: asserting a never-sized pipeline accepts `begin` moves the exit code to stage 25 on both arms.

**Three rows deferred with named owners** — `setSkybox`/`getSkybox` to `CBIND-091` and `getPassTimingsEXT` to `CBIND-089`, each blocked on a type that slice owns.

**A fourth row could not be bound at all, and the reason is an ABI-contract defect worth its own task.** `PostProcessContext::settings` needs a field appended to the published `CNA_PostProcessContext`. `ABI_VERSIONING.md` sanctions exactly that — "append fields to a versioned struct", and "CNA accepts only the fields contained in `struct_size`". **The implementation does not honour that contract for this struct.** Its validator tests `struct_size` for *exact equality*, so the moment the struct grows, every caller compiled against the old header is refused. Measured rather than assumed: 96 sites across the C API use the documented prefix rule (`struct_size <`), 15 more use `>=`, and **13 use exact equality** — `CNA_PostProcessContext` among them. Appending inside this slice would have been an ABI break dressed as a feature, and quietly changing thirteen validators inside a binding slice is not the same thing as fixing them deliberately. The row is deferred to `CBIND-100` with a named override.

Three arms, all passing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-089 | Bind the post-process chain and its passes | 233 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 at zero inventory rows.** All four child rows closed; the parent's own hypothesis — that the `CBIND-084` pass base would make each pass a create plus its own accessors — held, and the two helpers `CBIND-089B` wrote carried the last three slices unchanged. The one correction to the plan's own picture is that **two of the seventeen "passes" are not passes**; see `CBIND-089D`. | **Decomposed into `CBIND-089A`–`CBIND-089D` below; the parent closes only when all four rows and every `CBIND-089` inventory row are closed** — the rule `CBIND-084` through `CBIND-088` used. The count reads 233 rather than 232 because `CBIND-088B` deferred `RenderPipeline::getPassTimingsEXT` here, its return type being `PostProcessChain::PassTiming`.

**The row told its successor to test a hypothesis before writing code, and the hypothesis holds.** It said the pass base from `CBIND-084` should make each pass "a settings POD plus create/apply/destroy", and that if it did not, the right move was to fix `CBIND-084` rather than write seventeen bespoke shapes. Measured: **96 of the 233 rows are that shared base** — class, constructor, destructor, `apply`, `getName`, `isSupported`, six per pass — and `CBIND-084` already bound `apply`, `copy_name`, `is_supported` and `destroy` as operations on the `CNA_PostProcessPassHandle`. Those rows need a rule, not a route. Only **137 rows are pass-specific accessors**, and each pass needs exactly one new route beyond them: its `create`. No bespoke shapes, and no `CBIND-084` repair.

**Every pass corrects and almost none throw.** Of the seventeen, four throw at all — `PostProcessChain`, `DecalPass`, `SpatialUpscalePass`, `AsciiPass`, two each — and the corrections come in the same three shapes this phase has now met four times: `std::clamp` to a range, a floor via `std::max`/`AtLeast`, and the guarded assignment that keeps the previous value and that no idiom grep finds. Read each pass's bodies; do not carry a classification across passes.

The split is by **pass-specific** row count rather than total, because that is where the work is.
| CBIND-089A | Bind the post-process chain | 19 | ✅ | `PostProcessChain` (18) and `RenderPipeline::getPassTimingsEXT` (1), which `CBIND-088B` deferred here — the chain owns `PassTiming`, which is why the pipeline's timings row had to wait for it. Two throws. First of the four because every pass plugs into it, and because the timing surface it defines is how the other three slices' passes are read.

**Done 2026-08-27.** 17 routes; exports 3,308 → 3,325, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing. **`CBIND-088B`'s deferred row closes with it.**

**`addOwnedPass` is the one route in the engine layer that consumes a handle the caller still holds, and it needed a decision rather than a translation.** The canonical signature takes a `std::unique_ptr`; this ABI holds its objects in `shared_ptr`, which cannot release ownership — my first attempt invented a `release_for_transfer` that does not exist, and the compiler said so. Rather than force a transfer, the chain resource keeps the handed-over pass alive itself and registers it with the non-owning `addPass`. **The observable lifetime is identical**: the pass lives exactly as long as the chain, `clear` releases it, and the caller's handle is consumed either way — and it avoids the one thing a forced transfer risks, two owners of one object. The deviation is real and is recorded in the rule rather than hidden: canonically the chain's destructor frees the pass; here the chain's C resource does.

**The consumed handle is asserted, not documented.** A route that promises ownership transfer and leaves the handle usable invites a double free, so the test calls a route on the handed-over handle and requires `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_HANDLE`. That is the assertion broken to prove stage 26 executes, and it moved the exit code on both arms.

`PassTiming` splits into a plain `CNA_PassTimingEXT` value and a separate name copy, because a C structure cannot own the string and a structure with no lifetime of its own is the easier thing to reason about. Both canonical throws are argument mistakes answered before the chain sees them, with separate messages — a caller fixes a missing source and a zero size differently.

Three arms, all passing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-089B | Bind the screen-space passes | 56 | ✅ | `SsrPass` (22), `SsaoPass` (18), `DepthOfFieldPass` (16) — 38 pass-specific rows, the densest accessor surface in the phase. None throws; all three correct, and `SsrPass` alone uses two shapes (three clamps and three guarded assignments), so its bounds must be asserted one at a time the way `CBIND-088A`'s were.

**Done 2026-08-27.** 38 routes; exports 3,325 → 3,363, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing.

**The screening grep was wrong in the direction nobody watches for.** It reported five corrections in `SsaoPass`; reading the four setter bodies found **none** — all five live in `apply()`, computing sample positions. A grep that over-counts is as misleading as one that under-counts, and it would have produced four assertions for corrections that do not exist. `SsaoPass` corrects nothing, and the test says so.

**The bounds differ between fields of the same pass.** `SsrPass` clamps its roughness blur to `0..0.25` and its edge fade to `0..0.5` — proved to matter by asserting the edge fade takes its neighbour's bound, which moves the exit code to stage 27 on both arms. Each guarded setter gets the half a clamp does not need: a valid value first, then an out-of-range write that must leave the valid one in place.

**Two helpers now carry the shape for the rest of the phase.** `CreateEnginePass<T>` and `WithEnginePass<T>` are what make `CBIND-089C` and `CBIND-089D` a table rather than thirteen more designs; the second also fixes the refusal rule in one place — a pass-specific accessor called on another pass is refused **by argument**, because the handle is valid and it is the concrete type that cannot answer.

`kMinStepCount`/`kMaxStepCount` are bound as macros and the setter deliberately does not enforce them: the march clamps where it applies, so a caller reads back what it wrote. The test asserts a step count 500 past the documented maximum survives — the third time this campaign has met that convention.

Three arms, all passing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green.

**A flake worth naming rather than shrugging at.** The first full ON-tree run failed four unrelated suites — `CApi_BasicEffectSmoke`, `CApi_ModelSmoke`, `CApi_LifecycleSmoke`, `CApi_InputSnapshotsSmoke` — which then passed in isolation and on two consecutive full reruns, with the virtual display verified alive throughout so the earlier "dead `:101`" explanation does not apply here. That is the **third** occurrence of this shape in the phase, in a third unrelated set of suites. Recorded as `CBIND-101`; not diagnosed from three unreproduced samples, and no test changed on the strength of them. |
| CBIND-089C | Bind the atmospheric passes | 62 | ✅ | `AerialPerspectivePass` (17), `VolumetricFogPass` (15), `HeightFogPass` (15), `LightShaftPass` (15) — 38 pass-specific rows. None throws. `AerialPerspectivePass` has eight floors and one clamp, the most correction-dense body of the seventeen; `HeightFogPass` has floors and guarded assignments and **no clamp at all**, which is exactly the combination a `clamp` grep reports as "corrects nothing".

**Done 2026-08-27.** 39 routes; exports 3,363 → 3,402, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing. `CBIND-089B`'s two helpers were reused unchanged, which is what made four more passes a table rather than four more designs.

**The guards are not interchangeable, and that is the whole slice.** Four setters guard with `if (value >= 0)` and **accept zero** — no fog, no shafts and no aerial perspective are all legitimate settings — while `VolumetricFogPass::setRange` and `HeightFogPass::setFalloff` guard with `if (value > 0)` and reject zero as well, because a zero range has no volume to march and a zero falloff is a division by zero. A shared "negatives are ignored" reading would have got two of the six wrong. Proved to matter by asserting the range guard admits zero the way the density beside it does: the exit code moves to stage 28 on both arms.

**Two bounds worth naming.** `setTurbidity` floors at **one**, not zero — turbidity is a ratio against a perfectly clear atmosphere, so below one describes air clearer than vacuum. `setAnisotropy` clamps to **−0.95 through 0.95**, the only two-sided clamp in this phase with a negative lower bound, because scattering runs from fully backward to fully forward and both poles are singular. Neither is guessable from the field's name.

**A generator bug of mine, caught by the run rather than by review.** The stage generator's `else` branch swallowed `free` fields alongside the `> 0` guarded ones, so `HeightFogPass::setBaseHeight` — which corrects nothing — was given "rejects zero" assertions and failed at once. The generator now has an explicit `free` branch. Worth noting because the table was right and the code that consumed it was not: **a correct classification does not survive a generator that cannot express one of its cases.**

The three pure functions are checked by their **relationships** rather than by duplicating their maths: air mass grows with distance, transmittance falls as air mass grows, and optical depth is exactly zero at zero distance and grows with both distance and density.

Three arms, all passing. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-089D | Bind the remaining passes | 96 | ✅ | `BloomPass` (15), `DecalPass` (14), `LensFlarePass` (13), `MotionBlurPass` (11), `FxaaPass` (10), `SpatialUpscalePass` (10), `ChromaticAberrationPass` (8), `FilmGrainPass` (8), `AsciiPass` (7) — 46 pass-specific rows across nine passes: the largest row count and the smallest per-pass surface. `DecalPass`, `SpatialUpscalePass` and `AsciiPass` are the three here that throw. `FxaaPass` corrects nothing at all, which is worth asserting rather than assuming, on the `CBIND-087B` precedent.

**Done 2026-08-27.** 55 routes; exports 3,402 → 3,457, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing. **`CBIND-089` closes with it at zero rows.**

**Two of the nine are not passes, and the compiler is the only reason that was caught in time.** `DecalPass` and `SpatialUpscalePass` are named "Pass" and were counted among the seventeen, but neither derives from `PostProcessPass`: no `apply`, and each is driven by its own `draw` with explicit sizes rather than by a chain. Their `shared_ptr` would not convert, which is what surfaced it — **not** the row survey, which had counted them as ordinary passes with a shared base. They now carry their own handles, object kinds and destroy routes, and the shared `cna_post_process_pass_*` routes refuse them; the test asserts that refusal, because it is the only way a caller learns the difference from outside.

**A cross-translation-unit hazard, resolved rather than worked around.** `AsciiPass::getEffect` lends the pass's own effect, whose resource type lived in an anonymous namespace inside `CnaCApiGraphicsExt.cpp`. Two units that register or read one `ObjectKind` must agree on the type it holds; a second local declaration would have compiled, registered under the same kind, and then been cast to the wrong type — undefined behaviour no test would reliably catch. `AsciiEffectResource` moved to `CnaCApiGraphicsExtDetail.hpp`. That is a structural change made deliberately and named here, not smuggled into a binding commit.

**A silent partial edit, caught by the run.** Converting the two standalone objects off the shared handle, one of my replacements matched only the multi-line `draw` form, leaving four `SpatialUpscalePass` accessors still resolving `ObjectKind::PostProcessPass`. Every one of them returned `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_HANDLE` for a perfectly valid handle. Probing the value right after creation — rather than reasoning about where it might have been destroyed — found it in one step. **A textual replacement that reports no error has not reported success.**

`FxaaPass` corrects nothing while the settings bag that drives the same number floors it; both are asserted in the same stage, so the inconsistency is pinned rather than assumed away.

**`CApiRouteTestCoverage` caught one route with no caller**, `cna_decal_pass_draw` — bound, documented and matrix-green, but nothing exercised it. Now covered, including the sequencing refusal when the projector has a camera but no prepass inputs. Third time in the phase that gate has found a route the slice believed it had tested.

Three arms, all passing; proved to execute on both by asserting the shared destroy accepts a `DecalPass`. 96/96 in both trees, all nine gates green. |
| CBIND-090 | Bind HDR output, tonemapping and colour grading | 87 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-27.** 62 routes; exports 3,457 → 3,519, identical in both trees with zero symbols differing. 97/97 in both trees — the suite grew by one, the new oracle.

**The inheritance survey ran first this time, and that is the whole difference from `CBIND-089D`.** One command before any code: `TonemapPass` and `ColorGradePass` derive from `PostProcessPass`; `HdrDisplayOutput`, `CubeLut` and `AutoExposureEXT` do not. In `CBIND-089D` the compiler found that after the wrong shape was already built. A survey is cheaper than a compiler error and much cheaper than a shipped mistake.

**`CubeLut` is the layer's only byte-facing surface, and it is covered two ways as the release gate requires.** The oracle **emits where the parser scans** — opposite directions, no shared code — and the expected entry at any index is a closed form, so the comparison does not lean on the emitter's bookkeeping either. Every entry of every accepted size is compared, and one malformed case per refusal is enumerated. **Verified to catch a real disagreement rather than merely to run:** swapping red and blue in the parser's index arithmetic, the classic `.cube` ordering mistake, makes it exit 1 naming entry (1,0,0) at every size; restored, it exits 0. `tests/fuzz/CubeLutFuzz.cpp` covers what enumeration cannot, and judges the answer — an accepted table must report a size inside the documented bounds, read its corners, refuse one index past the edge and release.

**Three corrections here are unlike anything earlier in the phase.** `setPeakNits` floors against **another field**: a peak below diffuse white would make white brighter than the display's brightest, so the bound is the current paper-white and it *moves*. Proved to matter by asserting a fixed floor instead, which moves the exit code to stage 31 on both arms — no constant expectation could have caught it. `AutoExposureEXT` validates two of its setters **as pairs**, so one good value and one bad writes neither. And `setExposure` both refuses *and* corrects: non-positive is refused, and an accepted value is then clamped into the current range.

**`AutoExposureEXT` is compute-gated at construction**, the only object in the layer whose capability boundary is there rather than behind an `is_supported` query — its constructor builds a compute program and a storage buffer, so on HEADLESS there is no object to make and `create` answers `NOT_SUPPORTED`. Found by arm 3, not by reading.

**`TonemappingMode::Uncharted2` is bound**, closing what `CBIND-088A` found: the enumerator existed canonically but had no C name, so a caller could not select it and the settings validator refused its ordinal. The macro and the validator's bound moved together, which is what `CBIND-088A` deliberately deferred rather than widening a bound past a value nobody could spell.

**Three wrong classifications, all mine, all caught by running.** `ColorGradePass::setLut` and all four `AutoExposureEXT` setters were recorded as guarded assignments from a regex match on `if (…)`; every one of them **throws**. `setLut`'s `if` is an early return that *unbinds*, followed by a refusal for a strip whose width is not the square of its height. The amendment to the standing practice: **the classifier must distinguish `if (cond) throw` from `if (cond) field = value`** — they look identical to a pattern that stops at the condition, and they are opposite behaviours. The header, the routes and the test all said the wrong thing until the runs disagreed.

`CApiRouteTestCoverage` then caught six routes with no caller — the volume-LUT pair, both texture builders and both domain-corner readers. Fourth time in the phase that gate has found a route a slice believed it had tested. |
