| CBIND-091 | Bind image-based lighting, probes, sky and area-light shading | 146 → 0 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 at zero planned rows — but not on the first attempt.** `CBIND-091C` recorded this parent closed while two rows were still open, and the coverage gate caught it on the next run: `LightProbeEXT::operator==` and `operator!=` were bound by `cna_light_probe_ext_equals` all along, but the `light-probe` rule's `[A-Za-z0-9_~]` character class does not match `=`, so neither row was claimed. **This is the second time this exact character class has hidden operator rows in this campaign** — `CBIND-087B` hit it first — which is why the rule now spells `(?:operator[=!]=|[A-Za-z0-9_~]+)`. The lesson worth keeping is procedural rather than about regexes: the gate ran *before* the plan edit that declared the parent closed, so nothing re-checked the claim. A parent closure must be the last thing before a gate run, never after one. **The last `SYMBOL_OWNER_OVERRIDES` entry in the campaign expired here**: every deferral this phase recorded — two to `CBIND-087`, four and two to `CBIND-091A`, two to `CBIND-091B`, one to `CBIND-091C` — has been removed in the commit that bound it, which is the discipline `CBIND-086C` established. **Decomposed into `CBIND-091A`–`CBIND-091C` below; the parent closes only when all three rows and every `CBIND-091` inventory row are closed** — the rule every parent in this phase uses.

`LightProbeEXT`, `LightProbeVolumeEXT`, `LightProbeBaker`, `EnvironmentProcessor`, `Skybox`, `AtmosphericSky`, `AreaLightBrdfTable`, `AreaLightShading`, and `AreaLightEXT`/`ImageBasedLightEXT` from `modules/graphics`. `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT()` gates the whole slice; the baker is long-running and needs the campaign's settled answer for operations that are not instantaneous.

**137 base rows plus nine moved here by three earlier slices, and all three are named because the cell was wrong once already.** `CBIND-086C` moved **3** — `ClusteredForwardEffect::setAreaLight`, `setLightProbe` and `setLightProbeVolume`. `CBIND-087C` moved **4** — `setImageBasedLightEXT`/`getImageBasedLightEXT` on *both* `PbrEffect` and `SkinnedPbrEffect`. `CBIND-088B` moved **2** — `RenderPipeline::setSkybox`/`getSkybox`. 137 + 3 + 4 + 2 = **146**.

**The cell read 140 until 2026-08-27**: it was updated after `CBIND-086C` and then not after `CBIND-087C` or `CBIND-088B`, so it drifted by six while three slices each recorded their own deferral correctly in their own row. **A row-count cell that disagrees with the generator is exactly how `CBIND-079` began** — a number nobody re-derived, trusted because it had once been right. Whoever moves a row here updates this cell in the same commit, or the next reader inherits the same defect.

**Two surveys were run before any code, because the last three slices each cost something for skipping one.** *Inheritance:* none of the ten types derives from anything — no `PostProcessPass`, no shared base — so every one carries its own handle, and `AreaLightEXT`/`ImageBasedLightEXT` are `CNAEXT struct` PODs rather than classes. `CBIND-089D` learned that from the compiler and `CBIND-090` from a survey; this slice has the answer before the first route. *Contracts:* the throw counts are the highest in the phase — `EnvironmentProcessor` 9, `LightProbeVolumeEXT` 6, `LightProbeEXT` 5, `LightProbeBaker` 5 — so **every `if` gets read to its conclusion rather than pattern-matched**, which is what `CBIND-090` got wrong three times and `CBIND-099` got wrong four.

The split follows the dependency: the probe values first, because nine rows deferred here by three earlier slices all name them; then the producers that fill them; then area lighting, which shares nothing with either.

The seventh, `setAreaLight`, names a type `CBIND-091C` owns and was retargeted there rather than left pointing at the parent. 35 routes, stage 32, three arms, break-proved in both directions; exports 3,519 → 3,558, identical symbol-by-symbol in both trees.<br><br>**Two behaviours the slice bound as answers rather than refusals, because that is what the bodies do**: `visibilityWeight` returns **1.0** — "nothing known to be in the way" — both with no visibility data and at a non-positive distance, and `sampleProbe` **clamps** a position into the volume instead of refusing it. | CBIND-091C | Bind area-light shading | 37 → 38 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27**, and with it `CBIND-091`. `AreaLightBrdfTable` (17), `AreaLightEXT` with its `AreaLightShapeEXT` enum (14), `AreaLightShading` plus `ClusteredForwardEffect::setAreaLight` (8). 17 routes, stage 36, three arms, break-proved three ways; exports 3,612 → 3,629.<br><br>**`AreaLightEXT` is the second value type in this parent whose canonical home is the always-compiled XNA header**, so its `init`/`is_valid` pair works in every build — checked up front this time rather than discovered by a failing layer-absent arm, which is what `CBIND-091A` had to do.<br><br>**`IsValidEXT` is not restated in C**; the route rebuilds the canonical value and asks it. The test pins it with the case that isolates it — the *same* parallel axes read invalid as a rectangle, invalid as a disc, and valid as a tube — and breaking that answer moves both CNAEXT trees.<br><br>**`quadOf` is shape-dependent in two ways** and both are asserted: a disc scales its axes, and a tube is *billboarded*, so a tube's quad depends on the point being lit while a rectangle's does not. Asserting only a rectangle would have passed a binding that ignored the shape entirely.<br><br>**`setAreaLight` clears on a degenerate light rather than refusing it** — read from the body, not assumed; the slice's own header draft had said "refused" and was corrected before shipping. | CBIND-092A | Bind the particle system | 43 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27.** `ParticleSystem`, `ParticleEmitterSettings` and `Particle`, 24 routes, stage 37, three arms, break-proved both ways; exports 3,629 → 3,653, identical in both trees. 43 rows close.<br><br>**The contract worth the slice is that an unsustainable emission rate is not corrected on the way in.** `set_settings` assigns the struct whole and unvalidated, exactly as the canonical setter does; the clamp shows up in `get_active_count` and is reported by `is_emission_rate_clamped`. A binding that clamped in the setter would look friendlier and would make `get_settings` lie about what it was handed — so the test asserts a 200/s rate on a 64-slot system reads back *unchanged* while the count caps and the flag turns true, and separately that a rate filling the system *exactly* is not clamped, because the canonical test is strictly greater.<br><br>**Three corrections this slice made before shipping, all from reading rather than assuming.** (1) `usesCompute()` is written inside `update()` and nowhere else, so it reports what the **last update** did — forcing the CPU path does not change the answer until an update runs. The first draft's header said "says which path ran" and the first draft's test asked immediately after switching; both were wrong. (2) `CopyFormattedString` reports the text length **without** a terminator, and an empty string therefore *succeeds* at capacity zero rather than answering `BUFFER_TOO_SMALL` — which also means the five copy-route docs written across `CBIND-091A`–`CBIND-092A` saying "including the terminator" were wrong and are corrected here. (3) `CApiBoolContractSmoke` caught `set_simulation_on_cpu_ext` accepting any non-zero byte; `cna_area_light_shading_coverage` from `CBIND-091C` had the same defect and is fixed with it. Both now refuse a non-canonical `CNA_Bool` **before** resolving the handle, which is `CBIND-067`'s discipline.<br><br>Falling back to the CPU is a *success* path here, unlike GPU culling, and forcing it **carries the particles across** rather than restarting them — asserted by reading a particle before the switch and finding it unchanged after. |
| CBIND-092B | Bind instancing and LOD | 37 → 38 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27.** `InstancedRendererEXT` (18) and `LodGroupEXT` with its nested `Level` and the `LodSelectionMode` enum (20). 32 routes, stage 38, three arms, break-proved both ways; exports 3,653 → 3,685.<br><br>**The contract worth the slice is that a LOD group re-sorts its levels when the selection mode changes.** The two modes read a level's threshold in opposite directions — a distance threshold grows as detail falls, a pixel-radius threshold shrinks — so index zero only keeps meaning "finest" if the group re-sorts, which is also why the mode change forgets the last selection. A binding that stored the mode without re-sorting would pass every single-mode assertion and silently invert the order for anyone who switched; the test adds three levels out of order and reads them back across a mode change, and the break-proof confirms it fails when the order is asserted unchanged.<br><br>**Four behaviours here are answers rather than refusals**, each read from the body: an empty group selects `-1`, a negative distance is clamped to zero, a level may deliberately hold no part, and `projectedRadiusPixels` answers `FLT_MAX` at or behind the eye — "as large as it gets" picks the finest level rather than none. Against those, `setScreenSpaceParameters` is validated and refused as a **group**, with the field of view exclusive at both ends, and the test asserts pi itself is refused rather than clamped.<br><br>**The instanced renderer falls back rather than refusing**, and refuses only when the fallback is disabled *or* the effect cannot carry a per-instance transform — both `logic_error` canonically, both `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE` here. The capacity is asserted **never to shrink**, which is what makes a varying instance count allocation-free after the largest frame.<br><br>**Two shared helpers were added rather than duplicated.** `GetOwnedModelMeshPartValue` follows `GetOwnedSkinnedModelValue`'s precedent for reaching a resource whose layout is private to another adapter, and `ToCVertexElement` exports the vertex-element mapping so the engine layer describes the instanced renderer's static declarations with the same four-field conversion `CnaCApiVertexResources.cpp` already uses — two copies of that mapping would drift, and the drift would show up as an instance stream that almost works. The group hands back **the caller's own part handle** rather than wrapping the pointer, because a fresh handle would be a second owner of something the group only borrows. |
| CBIND-092C | Bind CPU and GPU culling | 36 → 37 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27.** `GpuInstanceCuller` with `GpuCullableInstance` (16), `FrustumCullerEXT` (10), and the two indirect-draw argument PODs (11). 23 routes, stage 39, three arms, break-proved three ways; exports 3,685 → 3,708.<br><br>**The two indirect-draw structs are the only PODs in this header without a `struct_size`/`struct_version` pair, deliberately**: the GPU reads them verbatim, so the layout *is* the contract and the ABI may not version or pad them. Compile-time assertions pin sixteen and twenty bytes against the canonical structs on both sides, and the test asserts the sizes from C as well. They live in the always-compiled graphics module, so both initializers work in every build.<br><br>**`cullTransforms` keeps a transform that has no matching bound** — the canonical test is `i >= bounds.size() || visible(bounds[i])`, so a short bounds array means "these ones are always visible", not "cull them". That is the opposite of what a caller who passed a short array by accident would expect, so the header states it and the test asserts three transforms with **zero** bounds return all three.<br><br>**`GpuInstanceCuller` refuses rather than falling back — the opposite of `CBIND-092A`'s particle system, and for a stated reason**: a CPU culling path would be a different algorithm producing a different visible set, so a device without compute gets an honest refusal instead of a silently different picture. Both arms are asserted, keyed on the measured support, and the break-proof shows they are genuinely distinct: inverting an EasyGL-only assertion moves `cmake-build-cnaext` and leaves `build-probe` green, because headless takes the refusal arm.<br><br>**Drawing before culling is `INVALID_STATE`, not `INTERNAL`.** The canonical body throws a `runtime_error`, which reaches the exception barrier's `std::exception` arm and would be reported as an internal failure — telling a caller it hit a library bug when it merely called two routes in the wrong order. The C layer mirrors the private `culled_` flag on exactly the two transitions the canonical class uses, and the test asserts four different `cull` refusals do **not** count as a cull.<br><br>One doc correction before shipping: `draw` issues a real indirect draw against **whatever mesh is bound**, so with none bound it is a native failure rather than a draw of nothing. The reachable success arm in a suite that binds no mesh is the zero-instance one, where the canonical body returns before touching the device; asserting a drawing draw would have asserted that the fixture had a mesh, not that the route works. |
| CBIND-092D | Bind debug drawing and the gizmos | 22 → 23 | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27**, and with it `CBIND-092`. `DebugDraw` and the six free gizmo functions, bound as routes on the same handle because they take a `DebugDraw&` as their first argument. 21 routes, stage 40, three arms, break-proved three ways; exports 3,708 → 3,729.<br><br>**There are no throws in the canonical class at all**, so the contracts worth testing are about *when* state is reset rather than about what is rejected. `begin` clears both line lists **and restores depth testing**, so a `set_depth_tested` before it is lost; `clear` discards the lines but leaves that choice alone; `end` is idempotent. The break-proof for the first of those moves both CNAEXT trees, which is what shows the reset is really being asserted rather than assumed.<br><br>**Each gizmo is asserted to add lines, not merely to succeed** — succeeding while drawing nothing is exactly the failure a result-code-only assertion misses — and two are pinned to an exact count: a probe volume is twelve box edges plus three per probe (asserting only the box fails), three cascades are twelve frustum edges each.<br><br>One assumption the layer-absent arm corrected: the block first asserted that the three light initializers refuse without the engine layer. | CBIND-093 | Bind the engine-layer surface on existing XNA and core types | 47 | ✅ | The `*EXT` methods the layer grafted onto types the C ABI already binds — `BasicEffect`, `SkinnedEffect`, `ShaderEffect` and `GraphicsDevice` — plus `CNA::SetAssemblyTitleEXT`/`GetAssemblyTitleEXT` and `AssemblyTitleAttributeEXT` in `modules/core`. These extend handles that already exist, so no new handle kind should appear here; the **three** `GraphicsDevice` capability queries (`ExecutesShaderEffectSourceEXT`, `SupportsShadowSamplingEXT`, `SupportsImageBasedLightingEXT`) are the rows the rest of the phase calls, so they land early even though this slice closes late. `SupportsComputeShadersEXT` is deliberately absent from that list: it is declared on `CNA::Internal::Renderers::IGraphicsRenderer`, which the inventory excludes by path, so it has no row here or anywhere — see the correction recorded on `CBIND-086`. A C caller asks `cna_graphics_device_supports_capability` with `CNA_GRAPHICS_CAPABILITY_COMPUTE_SHADERS`, which `GraphicsCapability.hpp` states is answered by that very method. |

**Closed 2026-08-27.** 17 new routes plus a rule-and-proof pass over 28 rows that needed none. Exports 3,729 → 3,746; **the matrix reaches zero planned rows.**

**28 of the 47 rows needed no new route at all.** The fourteen `cna_effect_*_ext` shadow-and-light routes refuse **by argument** through a `dynamic_cast` rather than by handle kind, so `BasicEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` are already served by the names `CBIND-087C` bound for the PBR effects. What remained was to *prove* they are among the accepted arguments — a rule claiming the rows without a test would be an assertion, not evidence. `BasicEffect` was already driven through all fourteen by the existing `validate_shadow_receiver`; stage 41 drives `SkinnedEffect` through the same fourteen.

**Ten of the twelve `GraphicsDevice` rows are not engine-layer routes at all** and live in `graphics_device.h` with their siblings, working in every build; only the two indirect draws need `engine_layer.h`, because their argument buffer is a `CNA_StorageBufferHandle`. The same split put the three `ShaderEffect` members in `effects.h` and the assembly title in `core_ext.h`. The layer-absent arm asserts each half for what it actually is — asserting the whole slice refuses would have been the easy mistake, and would have passed a build where the device queries had been misplaced.

**Three behaviours are answers rather than refusals**, each read from the body: an out-of-range compute axis answers **zero** (a caller looping over axes gets a usable number instead of an error to special-case); `SetDisplayColorSpaceEXT` **reports whether it worked** rather than refusing, because a display that cannot enter a space is an ordinary answer; and `SupportsDisplayColorSpaceEXT` is answered by **trying it and putting it back**, which the header states because a caller watching the display may see a momentary change.

**`AssemblyTitleAttributeEXT` maps to the setter rather than to a type of its own**, and the row records why: its whole purpose is to run the assignment at *static initialization* from a namespace-scope declaration, and C has no static-initialization construct to bind. A C caller sets the title from `main` — the same effect, reached the only way C can reach it.

Stage 41, break-proved three ways; nine gates green in both trees; 97/97 in all three.
| CBIND-094 | Make `CApi_MediaSmoke` and `CApi_GamersSmoke` hermetic | — | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-26.** Both tests read the ambient environment rather than one the registration controls: `CApi_MediaSmoke` writes under `$HOME` through the media library and fails outright where `$HOME` is not writable, and `CApi_GamersSmoke` opens an audio device and blocks where PulseAudio is unreachable. Both now get an isolated `XDG_DATA_HOME`/`HOME` under the build tree and `SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy`, unconditionally rather than only under `SDL_RENDERER`. A test whose result depends on the machine it runs on is not evidence, and these two were the reason a review of this branch had to hand-patch its environment before it could measure anything. |
| CBIND-097 | The shadow suites assume a renderer that supports shadows | — | ✅ | **Found by `CBIND-086A`'s third verification arm, fixed 2026-08-27. The recorded diagnosis was wrong, and re-deriving it rather than trusting it is the point of this row.**

The row said `ShadowMap::begin` sets `passOpen_` only when the map is supported, so `end` refuses. It does not: `begin` sets `passOpen_ = true` unconditionally on its last line, and `end` was never the failure. Instrumenting the suite on HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON` put the failure somewhere else entirely, and there were **two** defects, not one.

**First: a counted borrow taken unconditionally and released conditionally.** `CBIND-085B1`'s block calls `get_caster_effect`, `get_shadow_texture` and `get_skinned_caster_effect` on every renderer, but released them inside `if (supported == CNA_TRUE)`. The shadow texture is a render target that exists whether or not the caster shader links — measured, not assumed: on HEADLESS the two effect handles come back invalid and the texture handle comes back **valid**. So one borrow was taken and never given back, and `cna_shadow_map_destroy` was correctly refused for the rest of the suite. Releasing on *whether the getter handed back a real handle* — which is a different question from `supported` — fixes it, and as a side effect the "destroying is refused while a borrow is outstanding" assertion now runs on every renderer instead of only where the caster links. The spot, cascaded and cube blocks already branched on the handle and needed no change.

**Second: binding a cube face is not the same question as whether the caster shader links.** `CubeShadowMap::begin` throws out of `SetRenderTarget(cube, face)` on a renderer that refuses cube render targets; HEADLESS does, and the route reports `CNA_RESULT_INTERNAL`. The suite asserted `CNA_RESULT_SUCCESS`. It now branches on what `begin` actually returned — and **the refusing branch asserts more than the succeeding one**, rather than skipping: `end` must refuse because no pass was opened, and a second `begin` must fail *the same way it failed the first time* rather than with "a face pass is already open". That recovery invariant is exactly what the canonical `begin` marks the pass open after the bind to protect, and it is only observable on a renderer that refuses. A skipped test defends nothing; this one defends the thing the refusal makes visible.

The result code was left as it is. `CNA_RESULT_NOT_SUPPORTED` would arguably read better than `CNA_RESULT_INTERNAL` for a renderer that cannot bind a cube face, but changing it is a change to a landed slice's contract and belongs to a row that says so, not to a test fix.

**All three arms now pass end to end**, which they had never done: `cmake-build-debug` (layer off), `cmake-build-cnaext` (OPENGLES3/EasyGL), and `build-probe` (HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`). 96/96 in both full trees.

**The general lesson, which outlives the fix:** two arms were never enough. A slice verified on the renderer that supports its subsystem and on the build where the layer is absent has not tested the third case — the layer present on a renderer that cannot do the work — and that is where a subsystem's degrade-rather-than-fail contract actually lives. The corollary this fix adds: **a recorded diagnosis is evidence about the past, not a spec.** Both defects here were in code that had been read and written up once already; re-instrumenting found them in minutes, and following the written cause would have found neither. |
| CBIND-098 | `WeightedBlendedTransparency::begin` opens the bracket only where it is supported | — | ✅ | **Found by `CBIND-087D`, reproduced rather than fixed for two days, and fixed in the canonical engine layer on 2026-08-28 under an explicit owner exception to the audit-only rule.**

The defect: `begin` set `accumulating_` *after* `if (!isSupported()) return;`, so on a renderer that cannot run the resolve it bound nothing, left `isAccumulating()` false, and the matching `end()` threw `logic_error("accumulation is not open")`. A caller that bracketed its transparent geometry the obvious way got an error on the second call, on exactly the renderers least able to report why — and the object stayed unusable afterwards, because every later `begin` went down the same path.

**The fix is the `ShadowMap` shape (`plans/plan_modern.md` `MOD-1697`), not a new design.** `begin` now opens the bracket on every renderer and skips only the device work; a new `boundTargets_` records whether it actually bound anything, and `end` restores exactly what its `begin` changed. That second half matters and is not cosmetic: unconditionally resetting the blend and depth-stencil states in `end` would clobber states the caller set itself, on the one path where `begin` never touched them. A renderer that refuses the two-target bind is caught, leaves `boundTargets_` false and the bracket closed, and rethrows — so a refused bind costs one `begin`, not the object.

**The canonical suite was already failing and nobody had run it.** `WeightedBlendedTransparencyTest.EveryMisuseIsRejected` calls `begin`/`end` unguarded, so on `build-probe` (HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`) it failed four assertions and then threw out of the test body — a red test sitting in `CnaTests` while this campaign ran only the `CApi` suite. That is the finding behind the finding: a C API campaign that never runs the canonical suite cannot see the canonical defects it is binding.

**The new canonical test is `TheBracketIsSymmetricOnEveryRenderer`,** and it is deliberately not branched on `isSupported()` for the bracket itself — the contract is the same everywhere and the assertions are only *distinguishing* where the resolve is absent. It also pins the half the first `end()` does not cover: that a completed bracket leaves the object usable. Where it does branch is the blend state, and it compares a field the two states disagree on (`Additive`'s colour destination is `One`, `Opaque`'s is `Zero`) rather than the object's address, because the device stores a **copy** of the state rather than the caller's object.

**Mutation-checked rather than assumed.** With `if (!isSupported()) return;` restored ahead of the bracket and nothing else changed, `TheBracketIsSymmetricOnEveryRenderer` fails on the `end()` that must not throw and `EveryMisuseIsRejected` fails again; with the fix restored both pass. Both directions were run, not one.

**The C API test that pinned the defect now asserts the contract.** `EngineLayerSmoke.c` stage 23 used to require `is_accumulating` to equal `is_supported` and the unsupported `end` to refuse; both branches now assert that begin succeeds, `is_accumulating` reports true, and the matching end succeeds, with only the in-bracket behaviour differing. The unsupported branch additionally asserts the recovery the defect denied. The `weighted-blended-transparency` coverage rule's prose is corrected the same way — it described the defect as canonical behaviour — as is the `render-pipeline` rule's cross-reference to it.

**`CubeShadowMap` was checked and has no separate defect, which is a finding rather than an omission.** Its `begin` sets `passOpen_ = true` **unconditionally** on its last line; `supported_` guards only the caster-effect uniforms inside the `try`. What happens on HEADLESS is different in kind: `SetRenderTarget(cube, face)` throws because the renderer refuses cube render targets, so `begin` **fails** and a later `end` refusing is then correct and symmetric — which is what `CBIND-097` measured and what `EngineLayerSmoke.c` already asserts, including that a second `begin` fails the same way rather than reporting "already open". The distinguishing question is not "does the body branch on support" but "does `begin` report success and `end` then refuse", and only `WeightedBlendedTransparency` did. No task is opened for it.

**Verified in all three arms.** `cmake-build-debug` (HEADLESS, `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`, where every route refuses), `cmake-build-cnaext` (OPENGLES3/EasyGL, where the resolve runs) and `build-probe` (HEADLESS with `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`, the arm the defect lived on): 97/97 `CApi` each, and the canonical `WeightedBlendedTransparencyTest` suite green in both trees that compile it — 8 passing on EasyGL, 4 passing and 4 skipped for want of a half-float render target on HEADLESS.

**Not recorded in `plans/plan_modern.md`.** The engine layer's own plan makes no claim about this bracket that the fix falsifies, and inventing a `MOD-` number inside another campaign's sequence would be worse than the cross-reference above. |
| CBIND-099 | Three `ok = <expr>` sites can erase earlier failures | — | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27 as NOT A DEFECT. The premise of this row was wrong, and disproving it took one measurement.**

The row claimed three bare `ok = <expr>` sites in `EngineLayerSmoke.c` sat in blocks whose guard "does not test `ok`", so reaching one would discard every failure recorded before it. Widening the audit first found a **fourth** site of the same shape, which looked like confirmation. It was not.

**Every one of the four is unreachable when `ok` is false.** Three are preceded by `if (!ok) { …; return 0; }`, which bails out before the block; the fourth sits inside an enclosing `if (ok) { … }`. The original scan looked only at the *immediately* enclosing `if` and never at the control flow around it, so it saw a guard that does not mention `ok` and concluded the block was reachable with `ok` false.

Disproved empirically rather than by re-reading: on the **pre-fix** file, breaking the assertion directly above the last remaining candidate makes stage 15 fail (exit 18). A site that masked would have swallowed it and exited 0.

The four assignments are now `ok = ok && …` anyway. That is **hardening, not a fix**: it costs nothing, and it means a future edit that removes one of those guards cannot quietly reintroduce the hazard. The row is closed as not-a-defect rather than as fixed, because saying "fixed" would leave the next reader believing four real masks once existed.

**What was actually wrong, and it is mine.** `CBIND-087D` found a genuine masked assertion — in a branch with no `if (ok)` protection at all, demonstrated by watching one arm move and the other not. Generalising from that one real case to three others by pattern-matching, without checking whether the pattern could fire, is the same error as `CBIND-090`'s `if (cond) throw` misclassification: **a pattern was matched and its reachability never tested.** Two instances in three slices is a habit, not an accident. The rule that follows: a defect found by inspection is a hypothesis until a run distinguishes the broken behaviour from the correct one — and for a masking defect that means producing the failure it is supposed to swallow. |
| CBIND-100 | Thirteen validators test `struct_size` for exact equality, so their structs cannot grow | — | ✅ | **Found by `CBIND-088B`, which needed to append a field and could not.** `docs/c-api/ABI_VERSIONING.md` documents the growth path outright — "Append fields to a versioned struct", and "CNA accepts only the fields contained in `struct_size`; new fields are appended at the end. … a future larger struct is accepted only after its known prefix is validated." **Thirteen validators do not implement that.** They test `struct_size != sizeof(...)`, which refuses both an older caller's smaller struct and a newer caller's larger one, so any one of these structs that ever grows breaks every binary compiled against the previous header.

Measured, not assumed: 96 sites use the documented prefix rule (`struct_size <`), 15 use `>=`, and 13 use exact equality — `CNA_SpriteCommand`, `CNA_SpriteScaledCommand`, `CNA_RenderTargetBinding`, `CNA_SpriteFontGlyph`, `CNA_PostProcessContext`, `CNA_PbrMaterialEXT`, `CNA_DirectionalLightEXT`, `CNA_SpotLightEXT`, `CNA_PointLightEXT`, `CNA_ShadowCascadeStateEXT`, `CNA_PunctualLightEXT`, `CNA_ClusteredLightEXT` and `CNA_RenderPipelineSettingsEXT`. Nothing is broken today, because none of them has grown yet; the defect is that the first one to grow breaks its callers, and the documentation promises otherwise.

Two of these are mine — `CNA_ClusteredLightEXT` (`CBIND-086A`) and `CNA_RenderPipelineSettingsEXT` (`CBIND-088A`) — written to match the exact-equality neighbours around them rather than the contract. That is worth naming rather than quietly fixing: **the wrong pattern propagates by imitation, which is why it reached thirteen sites.**

The work: change all thirteen to validate a known prefix, add a test that passes a *smaller* `struct_size` and requires the prefix to be honoured (today's suites only ever pass the exact size, which is why none of them caught this), then append the settings field to `CNA_PostProcessContext` and close `CBIND-088`'s last row. Do not fold the validator change into another slice — it touches four subsystems and deserves its own verification.

**Done 2026-08-27. No new routes and no export change — 3,457 before and after.** All thirteen now use the documented prefix rule (`struct_size < sizeof(T)`), which is the idiom the other 109 sites already used; the two written by this campaign, `CNA_ClusteredLightEXT` and `CNA_RenderPipelineSettingsEXT`, were among them.

**Loosening the rule is not the same as making a structure growable, and the difference is where the real work was.** `struct_size < sizeof(T)` accepts a *larger* structure from a future caller, but it still refuses a *smaller* one — and a smaller one is exactly what an existing caller sends once the structure grows. So `CNA_PostProcessContext` also gained an explicit mandatory prefix, `CNA_POST_PROCESS_CONTEXT_SIZE_V1`, and its validator measures against that rather than against the current `sizeof`. Without it the phrase "a structure may grow" would have stayed true only in the documentation.

**`CBIND-084C`'s deferred field is bound, which closes `CBIND-084`.** `PostProcessContext::settings` is appended as version 2 and mapped as a **borrowed pointer**, matching the canonical `const RenderPipelineSettings*` rather than copying a two-hundred-byte value into every context. A version-1 caller's structure stops before it and is accepted; the resolver reads the field only when `struct_size` says the caller allocated it, which is the whole point of the prefix rule stated as code.

**Why no test caught this for thirteen structures.** Every suite passed the exact current size — the one value that both the correct rule and the broken one accept. The defect was invisible not because the tests were weak but because they all sat on the single point where the two rules agree. Stage 30 now passes a version-1 size, a larger-than-current size, and a size one byte below the prefix, and asserts accept/accept/refuse. Proved by restoring the exact-equality check and watching the exit code move to stage 30: **the test that has to exist before growth is safe, not after it.** |
| CBIND-101 | Unrelated `CApi_*` suites fail together in a full run and pass on rerun | — | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-28 as NOT REPRODUCIBLE under the expanded multi-target integration protocol, on the project owner's authorisation — not as fixed, and not with a cause invented for it.**

Five occurrences across the phase, in six unrelated suites, none reproducible in isolation: `CApi_MediaPlayerSmoke` (before `CBIND-084`); `CApi_SensorDeviceSmoke` (`CBIND-086C`); `CApi_BasicEffectSmoke`, `CApi_ModelSmoke`, `CApi_LifecycleSmoke` and `CApi_InputSnapshotsSmoke` together (`CBIND-089B`); `CApi_AudioSmoke` (`CBIND-092A`); and `CApi_RuntimeComponentsSmoke` (this row, in the first post-merge integration run).

**What the earlier rows had already ruled out.** Five consecutive full runs against an unchanged, already-built tree: 97/97 five times, so repetition of the suite is not the trigger. Four build-then-run cycles where a single target was relinked immediately before the suite: 97/97 four times, so a one-target rebuild is not it either. `ctest` was never given `-j`, so parallel execution never fit.

**The one variant left was a build of many targets across all three trees followed immediately by the serial suite, and this merge required exactly that.** Five such cycles were run — a whole-configuration `cmake --build` of `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe` (1,209 / 1,935 / 1,339 targets), each followed immediately by `ctest -R '^CApi' --output-on-failure` with no `-j`, in each of the three trees. **Fifteen full serial runs.** Every unexplained failure count: **one**, and it was in the first cycle.

| Cycle | What preceded it | debug | cnaext | probe |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | full build of all three trees (post-merge), **ambient display** | 91/97 | 91/97 | 91/97 |
| 2 | full rebuild of all three trees, display forced | 94/97 | 94/97 | 94/97 |
| — | no build; documents regenerated | 97/97 | 97/97 | 97/97 |
| 3 | full rebuild (the `CBIND-098` fix), display forced | 96/97 | 96/97 | 96/97 |
| 4 | full rebuild, display forced | 97/97 | 97/97 | 97/97 |
| 5 | full rebuild, **display pinned per test, nothing forced on the command line** | 97/97 | 97/97 | 97/97 |

Every failure in cycles 1–3 was identified and is accounted for: three generated-document gates the merge had made stale (`CApiCoverageMatrix`, `CApiLimitations`, `CApiReleaseGate`), and three real merge defects fixed in the merge commit (`CApi_LifecycleSmoke`, `CApi_ContentSmoke`, `CApi_TextureVolumeSmoke`). The one that is not accounted for is `CApi_RuntimeComponentsSmoke` in cycle 1's `cmake-build-cnaext` run, which passed in isolation immediately afterwards — with and without the forced environment — and in all four later cycles, having been neither rebuilt nor edited in between. That is the fifth occurrence of the shape, and it is the whole of what fifteen runs produced.

**Its complete output was captured before any rerun, and the capture is the finding.** It is the renderer banner and nothing else: `RuntimeComponentsSmoke.c` contains **no `printf` or `fprintf` at all** and reports only through exit codes, so a `--output-on-failure` capture of it can never say more than "it failed". Several suites are like this. `CBIND-113` is opened for that, because the cheapest thing that would move this row further is making the *next* occurrence informative rather than running the loop again.

**A structural candidate was found, and it is recorded as a candidate.** Every one of the six suites in every occurrence scopes its `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy` to `if(CNA_GRAPHICS_RENDERER STREQUAL "SDL_RENDERER")`. None of this phase's three arms is `SDL_RENDERER`, so in all of them those tests named no video driver and inherited `ctest`'s own environment — on this host a live Wayland desktop session, plus a cached `CNA_TEST_DISPLAY` of `:0` that survived every reconfigure that did not name it. A shared resource another application can resize, focus, lock or blank underneath a test is exactly the kind of thing that fails once in fifteen runs. **The population matches perfectly — six suites out of six — and that is not the same as a cause.** Reproducing it would mean deliberately driving the owner's desktop, which is precisely what must not be done, so it stays a candidate.

**What was changed is justified without it.** Every C API test that names no video driver now gets `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11` and the configured `CNA_TEST_DISPLAY`, appended to whatever environment it already had; a test that names its own driver — `dummy`, or `offscreen` for the engine layer — is untouched. That is `CBIND-094`'s rule applied to the display rather than to `$HOME`: a test whose result depends on the machine it runs on is not evidence. Cycle 5 proves the pinning stands on its own, with nothing forced on the `ctest` command line: 97/97 in all three arms.

**Not closed as fixed.** Fifteen runs that do not reproduce a defect are not a diagnosis, and the environment change is hardening rather than a repair of something demonstrated broken. If it recurs, the first thing it needs is `ctest -VV` and `LastTest.log` — which records the exit code the suite's own output cannot — and `CBIND-113`'s stage prints.

Evidence: the `CBIND-086C`, `CBIND-089B` and `CBIND-092A` rows in this plan, and this session's fifteen `ctest` logs. |
| CBIND-095 | Close the reopened matrix | — | ✅ | **Closed 2026-08-27.** `CBIND-080`–`CBIND-093` are all ✅, the inventory has **0 planned rows**, `RELEASE_GATE.md` reads **ready**, the ABI baseline is regenerated and all five prose export counts agree with it at 3,746 — the same 3,746 in both builds, measured symbol by symbol.

**This row forbade closing on a green `--check`, so four independent checks were run instead of one.** Each targets a way a previous closure turned out to be an illusion:

1. **No rule describes a route that does not exist.** All 98 Phase B9 rules were parsed for the `cna_*` names their mapping and test text cite, and every one was matched against the shared library's actual export list. Zero missing. (`CBIND-052A`'s failure was a row recorded implemented with no C constant existing at all.)
2. **No approval is stale.** Every Phase B9 rule's `approved_symbols` was re-derived from the current headers with the tool's own matcher and compared against what is recorded. Seventeen looked like drift and all seventeen were explained rather than waved away: fifteen are symbols another rule claims by precedence, and two are **deleted** operations, which `map_symbols` classifies before matching ever runs — the re-derivation script did not model that branch. No recorded approval differs from what the headers say today. (`CBIND-050`'s failure was approvals seeded from a stale tree.)
3. **The rows were bound, not reclassified.** Phase B9 owns **16 of the 459** not-applicable rows — all in `CBIND-080`, `083`, `084A` and `084C`, each with its reason recorded in its own row — and added **zero** partials: all 15 partial rows still belong to `CBIND-036`, `037`, `069` and `074`. The 1,396 reopened rows became C routes, not dispositions. This is the check that would have caught a matrix closed by relabelling.
4. **Unmatched means planned, not silently accounted for.** Confirmed by reading `map_symbols`: a symbol no rule claims falls through to `status="planned"` with an owner task, so a rule that quietly stopped matching would reopen the matrix rather than hide in it.

**What is *not* claimed by this closure.** The gate governs the **experimental** `0.9.0` ABI; ABI 1.0 is a separate decision it does not make. Two defect rows remained open by design at the time — `CBIND-098` (reproduced, not fixed, per the owner's standing rule then in force) and `CBIND-101` (an intermittent full-run test failure needing a reproduction loop) — and neither is a coverage row, so neither blocked this. Both were closed on 2026-08-28. |

## Phase B10 — the CNB content format, and the tail the sixth merge left behind

The matrix reopened for the sixth time, on 2026-08-28, by **506 rows**. As with `CBIND-079`, most
of it is one thing: **460 rows are `CNA::Content::Cnb`**, the CNB content format
(`plans/plan_cnb.md`'s `CNBF-002`–`CNBF-123`), which arrived as 23 public headers under
`modules/content/include/CNA/Content/Cnb/`. The other 46 are ordinary XNA symbols the same merge
added beside it.

**The owner's decision, 2026-08-28, is what makes this a phase rather than a session.** Asked
whether to bind the new surface now, the ruling was: *there is no time for it in this pass — record
that CNB is not currently supported in the C bindings API so the tests pass, write the CNB tasks
into this plan, finish the merge, and delegate the rest to the future.* So Phase B10 is a **real,
sized, unstarted backlog**, and the C ABI's published state says so rather than implying otherwise.

Two consequences follow from that decision, and both are deliberate:

- **The experimental release gate reads `Not ready`,** on exactly one criterion — *No public C++
  symbol is unaccounted for* — and on no other. That is the gate working. `CBIND-042B` built it to
  fail in both directions precisely so a deferral shows up in the published verdict instead of
  being remembered by whoever deferred it. Do not "fix" the verdict by reclassifying rows.
- **No row is dispositioned to make a number look better.** Every one of the 506 stays `planned`
  and names an open task below. `CBIND-047` excluded `modules/platform` as a substrate and that was
  a *scope* decision the owner made; this is a *scheduling* decision, and the two must not be
  confused. Marking the CNB surface `not-applicable` would say C cannot express it, which is false:
  it is a byte format with PODs and free functions, and it is one of the more straightforwardly
  bindable things in this repository.

### What a B10 slice inherits

Everything in *Working practice for every remaining slice* and *The loop for one slice* still
applies unchanged. Three things are specific to this phase:

- **CNB is not conditional.** Unlike the CNAEXT engine layer, these headers are compiled in every
  configuration, so a slice needs no `#else` refusal arm and no both-ways build to prove one. What
  it still needs is all three verification arms, because the *content* paths differ per renderer.
- **The format is versioned on disk, and the ABI must not re-version it.** `CnbFormat` already
  carries schema numbers and a container version; a C route reports them, it does not invent a
  parallel versioning scheme. The published `struct_size`/`struct_version` rule governs the C
  structures only.
- **Read the refusals before writing a route.** The CNB readers refuse a great deal on purpose —
  `CNBF-114`–`CNBF-121` turned six conventions into boundaries — and every one of those is a
  contract to preserve as a `CNA_RESULT_*`, not a clamp. `CnbReadLimits` is the type that states
  most of them.

| # | Task | Rows | Status | Acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| CBIND-102 | Integrate `origin/next`, restore a consistent inventory, and give every new planned row an owner | — | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** The merge itself is commit `Merge origin/next into feature/bindings`; this row is the record that follows it. `B10_SLICE_OWNERS` in `tools/c-api/generate_coverage_inventory.py` partitions all 506 reopened rows onto `CBIND-103`–`CBIND-111` by module and header stem, and `COVERAGE.md`, `LIMITATIONS.md` and `RELEASE_GATE.md` are regenerated from the merged tree. Three of the new keys — `graphics/DynamicVertexBuffer`, `graphics/VertexBuffer` and `content/ContentManager` — already named a Phase B9 slice recorded ✅, which is the exact state `CBIND-079`'s gate forbids; the new partition is consulted first so the finished slice stops answering for work it never saw. Verified by running that gate: before the partition it reported `CBIND-035`/`036`/`080`/`083` owning 10/475/19/2 planned rows, and after it reports none. The inventory is **536 headers / 8,812 symbols — 7,832 implemented, 15 approved partial, 506 planned, 459 not applicable**, and the release gate reads **Not ready** on the one criterion those 506 rows fail.

**Verified with `CBIND-095`'s five checks before the branch was pushed, because a reopening can be dishonest in the same ways a closure can.** The first four are that row's; the fifth is Phase B9's ABI promise.

1. **No mapping rule cites a route that does not exist.** 528 distinct `cna_*` names across all 609 rules, held against the shared library's own export list. One name is not exported — `cna_color_init` — and the rule citing it is `color-default-constructor`, whose prose says a `cna_color_init` route *would add a second spelling for what C already expresses in a declaration*. A rule naming a route it argues should not exist is the one case this check must not flag, so it is read rather than counted.
2. **No approval over-claims.** Re-deriving every rule's `approved_symbols` with the tool's own matcher, 27 rules disagree — **all** of them in the direction "recorded but not measured", never once the reverse, which is the direction that would matter. 16 are `=delete`d copy constructors, which `map_symbols` classifies as not-applicable *before* matching runs, so the re-derivation cannot attribute them (the same modelling gap `CBIND-095` recorded when it was two). The other 22 name symbol IDs that exist in no inventory at all — **including the pre-merge one**, checked against `COVERAGE.md` at the merge's first parent — so they are stale IDs inherited from older trees rather than anything this merge moved. A stale ID matches nothing and cannot bless anything; they are left as they are rather than bulk re-approved, which is what `CBIND-050` warns against.
3. **Nothing was reclassified to shrink the backlog.** Phase B10 owns **0 of the 459** not-applicable rows and **0 of the 15** partials — both sets belong entirely to earlier tasks — and **all 506** planned rows name a B10 slice, with none naming a task outside the phase. This is the check that would catch a matrix "closed" by relabelling, run here against a matrix being *opened*, where the same temptation runs the other way.
4. **Unmatched still means planned.** Measured rather than read: removing one rule (`graphics-renderer-identities`) moves exactly its 51 symbols from implemented to planned, 7,832→7,781 and 506→557. A rule that quietly stopped matching would reopen the matrix rather than hide in it.
5. **Both `CNA_CNAEXT` configurations still export the same set, symbol by symbol.** 3,746 names in `cmake-build-debug` (OFF), `cmake-build-cnaext` (ON, OPENGLES3) and `build-probe` (ON, HEADLESS); `diff` of the three sorted lists is empty. Declared-versus-exported agrees exactly at 3,746 in each, and the ABI baseline verifies against each library. |
| CBIND-103 | Bind the math tail | 7 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** All 7 rows close with **no new routes and no ABI change** — the first slice in this phase where the right answer was that the surface already existed: implemented 8,291 → 8,298 and planned 46 → 39, exports unchanged at 4,018 and the ABI still `0.15.0`.

**Both checks the row demanded were performed, and both passed — but they were performed.** `cna_matrix_multiply` and `cna_matrix_multiply_scalar` do take `CNA_Matrix` **by value**, so `M *= N` is `cna_matrix_multiply(m, n, &m)` and that is well defined rather than an aliasing hazard, exactly as it is for `Vector2`. A 64-byte value was the plausible place for the convention to have changed, and it did not. `Quaternion()` really is all zeros — `X(0), Y(0), Z(0), W(0)`, with the canonical comment recording that C# zeroes struct fields — so the C form is the zero-initialized `CNA_Quaternion` a caller writes in a declaration, and the friendlier-looking identity is **not** it.

**The tests measure both properties rather than restating them, and both were mutation-checked.** Each aliasing case runs twice — once into a separate destination, once with the destination aliasing an operand, from **both** sides — and then requires the aliased result to differ from the input, so a match cannot be a no-op agreeing with itself. Giving the aliased matrix call a different scalar fails; setting the zeroed quaternion's `w` to 1 fails. That is a stronger bar than `CBIND-081` set for `Vector2`, and it is the bar this row asked for.

**One thing the approvals machinery caught that a regex would not have.** `^Quaternion::Quaternion$` matches every constructor overload, and `quaternion-operations` already owns the parameterised ones; the deriving script's own overlap assertion fired rather than silently approving all of them. The rule now carries `signature_regex: ^\(\)$`, so its pattern says what it means instead of relying on the approval list to fix it afterwards. **`color-default-constructor` has the same latent looseness** — a broad pattern held correct only by its single approval — which is worth a look under `CBIND-112` rather than a change here.

**Verified in all three arms**, each built whole and then run serially: 103/103 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe`; all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 4,018 in each tree. |
| CBIND-104 | Bind the graphics tail | 22 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** Five routes, no new structures and no new handle kinds, close all 22 rows: implemented 8,298 → 8,320 and planned 39 → 17, ABI `0.15.0` → `0.16.0` and exports 4,018 → 4,023.

**The `using` question is answered, and the answer is that a `using` declaration introduces no operation, so it gets no route.** The fourteen are `VertexBuffer`'s own seven typed families appearing on the derived type because `using VertexBuffer::SetData;` makes the base overloads declarations of it too. The C shape already carried the reason and it is now written down: **a dynamic buffer is not a separate handle type here** — `CNA_VertexBufferCreateInfo` has a `dynamic` flag and `CNA_VertexBufferInfo` reports it, so one `CNA_VertexBufferHandle` addresses both kinds and `cna_vertex_buffer_set_data` serves both. The transfer descriptor also carries `options`, which is why the same route answers these no-option overloads *and* the `SetDataOptions` overloads the derived type declares itself. They get their own rule rather than joining `vertex-buffer-complete-contract`, because that rule's text describes the base contract and what needed saying is a different statement.

**Why they were `planned` at all is worth recording:** `vertex-buffer-complete-contract` is a `.*` rule whose `header_regex` already covers `DynamicVertexBuffer.hpp`, so the pattern always matched them — they fell through because the merge added the declarations and nothing had approved them. That is exactly the `CBIND-050` scenario the approval half of the matcher exists for, working as designed.

**The three templates and the two protected methods beneath them needed two real routes.** `cna_vertex_buffer_set_data_raw_with_options` and `_raw_at_with_options`: the existing raw pair takes a stride but no streaming hint, and `SetDataRawWithOptions`/`SetDataRawAtWithOptions` are protected, so the templates are their only public door. Both new routes call the **windowed** template, because it takes the stride as an argument while the narrower one derives it from `sizeof(TVertex)` — and a stride known only at run time cannot be a type's size. The two-argument template is answered by the existing `cna_vertex_buffer_set_data_raw`. **A static buffer is refused**, since these overloads are declared on `DynamicVertexBuffer` and a streaming hint means nothing without one; the test measures that refusal, because a route that quietly accepted a static buffer would be inventing a path the canonical API does not have.

**`EffectMaterial` has no handle of its own, so both routes check what they were given.** It is a `CNA_EffectHandle` whose object happens to be an `EffectMaterial` — which is what `cna_effect_material_create` hands back — so a plain Effect is refused rather than trusted. The texture arrives as a handle plus the `CNA_EffectTextureType` discriminator `cna_effect_parameter_set_value_texture` already uses; a null handle is ignored rather than refused, because the canonical method ignores a null pointer and the two must not disagree about the same input.

**The lifetime decision, which `SAMPLE-028` is the reason for: retaining deliberately does *not* gate the texture's own destroy.** `cna_effect_parameter_set_value_texture` gates one because a parameter holds a raw pointer nothing owns; retaining is precisely the mechanism that removes that hazard. So only the canonical texture is handed over, and the test destroys the caller's handle and checks the count stays raised — the arm a gate would have made unreachable. Mutation-checked: removing the retain call fails.

**One test guard removed on purpose.** The texture create was first written inside an `if`, which would have let the whole retained-texture contract stop being checked on any build that could not make a 1×1 texture. HEADLESS makes one — verified by instrumenting the branch rather than assuming — so the create is now required, because a skipped check defends nothing.

**Throw/clamp survey: `VertexBuffer.cpp` 51 throws and 0 clamps; `EffectMaterial.cpp` and `OcclusionQuery.cpp` 0 and 0.** Nothing here had to choose between a canonical refusal and a softer C answer.

**A second regex looseness caught the same way `CBIND-103`'s was.** The template overloads carry their `template<typename TVertex>` prefix in the recorded signature, so the first pattern matched only 19 of the 22 rows and the deriving script said so instead of writing a short approval set. The rule's `signature_regex` now names all five forms explicitly.

**Verified in all three arms**, each built whole and then run serially: 103/103 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe`; all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 4,023 in each tree. |
| CBIND-105 | Bind the reflective content readers and the `.cnb` loader hook | 17 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** Ten routes over one new handle kind close all 17 rows: implemented 8,320 → 8,337 and **planned 17 → 0**. ABI `0.16.0` → `0.17.0`, exports 4,023 → 4,033.

**A pointer-to-member becomes a kind and a byte offset.** The canonical builder's `Field(&T::member)` captures a member pointer, which C cannot name; the same information is a field's *kind* and *where it goes in the object*, and that is what `add_field` takes. Chaining becomes a handle plus append routes, following `cna_content_type_reader_manager_register` as the row asked rather than inventing a mechanism.

**Two things the C form must supply that C++ took from `T`, and both are decisions rather than transcriptions.** The object is made by a **caller callback**, because the canonical reader default-constructs its `T` — and that settles ownership without an allocator crossing the ABI: the object is allocated and freed by the caller, and CNA only writes the declared fields into it. And a **reference-type member cannot land in a byte offset at all**, because XNA writes it with its own reader index in front; `add_custom` is what reads one, and the header says so rather than leaving a caller to find out.

**`ReflectiveTypeReader<T>` is not instantiated and could not be.** It needs a C++ `T` to default-construct and to hold member pointers into. What is bound is the **contract** — the canonical reader name, the declared wire order, value types read inline — implemented in the C layer against a caller-made object. Recording that plainly is better than implying a template was reached from C.

**`Register()` returns no handle, because the canonical one has no undo.** Registering the same name again replaces the entry, which is what both sides rely on; a withdrawal route would be surface the canonical layer does not have.

**The row's premise about the `.cnj` sibling does not hold, and that is the finding.** `cna_content_manager_register_cnj_loader_ext` is **per-manager**, because `RegisterCnjLoader<T>` is a non-static member; `RegisterCnbLoaderEXT` is **static** and process-wide. Matching the sibling's shape would have given a process-wide registration a manager argument it does not use. And the canonical method's whole body forwards to `CnbLoaderRegistry::Register` after dropping the asset name — which the C callback already receives — so it maps to `CBIND-111`'s route with **no second spelling published**.

**What `CBIND-111` left open is now settled by measurement rather than argument.** That row said a C-registered CNB loader is invoked directly rather than reached through a manager. It is reached through a manager: `validate_cnb_loader_through_manager` writes a real custom-typed `.cnb`, registers a C loader for its type, and loads it **by asset name through `cna_content_manager_load_foreign_ext`**. Mutation-checked by registering the loader under a different asset type, which makes the load fail.

**The reflective reader is proved against a hand-written `.xnb`, not a mock**, whose type-reader table is built **from the canonical-name routes** rather than transcribed — so a change to the name composition breaks the file instead of passing unnoticed. Three mutations were run: expecting the custom callback's undoubled value fails; dropping the enum-reader registration fails, which is what proves an `.xnb`'s table must resolve in full before any object is read even for a reader nothing dispatches to; and registering the CNB loader for the wrong type fails.

**One mutation check that did not mutate, caught and redone.** The first attempt used a `str.replace` with no assertion and silently changed nothing, so the "failure" it reported was a pass. The second attempt failed to compile under `-Werror` and re-ran the **stale binary**. Only the third — registering under a different asset type, which compiles — actually bit. A mutation check that does not mutate proves exactly as much as a skipped test.

**The route-coverage gate found a real gap.** `cna_enum_type_reader_get_canonical_name_size` was published and never called; the gate refused the slice until a test named it. That is the gate doing the job the plan gave it — a matrix row saying `implemented` is not evidence anything calls the route.

**The matrix is closed: 0 planned rows, and `docs/c-api/RELEASE_GATE.md` now reads `Ready` on all ten criteria.** That verdict is *measured*, not asserted, and it is **not** this row's closure claim: `CBIND-112` still owes the four independent checks, and its own rule says not to close on a green `--check`.

**Verified in all three arms**, each built whole and then run serially: 103/103 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe`; all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 4,033 in each tree. |
| CBIND-106 | Bind the CNB container: identities, format, arithmetic, CRC-32C and read limits | 66 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** `CNA/C/cnb.h` and `CnaCApiCnb.cpp` add 24 routes, two identities, one versioned structure and 30 constants, closing all 66 rows: implemented 7,832 → 7,898 and planned 506 → 440, the delta this phase requires to equal the slice's row count.

**The family owns no handle, and saying so is a design decision rather than an accident.** Everything here is a pure function over caller-owned bytes plus `CNA_CnbReadLimits`, so there is no lifetime to manage, no thread affinity and no `_destroy`. `ObjectKind`'s next free number is untouched. Nothing in `CNA::Content::Cnb` has an XNA 4.0 counterpart, so no route carries an `_ext` suffix — `core_ext.h`'s rule that a whole header of CNA-namespace surface is its own marker, applied to the two canonical members (`Crc32cUsesHardwareEXT`, `Crc32cPortableEXT`) that do carry one.

**Three flattenings, each replacing several rows rather than adding routes.** `CnbChunkId` is a structure holding exactly one `uint32_t` with defaulted equality, so `CNA_CnbChunkId` is that `uint32_t` and maps the structure, its field and its `operator==` together — C's own `==` is the comparison. `CnbCompression`'s three `Reserved*` enumerators are **aliases of the same values**, not distinct ones, so they are the same constants; a second C name for one wire value would invite a caller to believe the two mean different things in a file. And `Crc32cSeed()` is a `constexpr` function returning a literal, which in C is a constant — usable in a static initializer, which a route would not be.

**The `level` parameter is a correction and the two refusals are contracts, decided by reading the bodies first.** `CompressCnbChunk` clamps `level` to the codec's range, so the C route clamps too and documents it rather than refusing. `DecompressCnbChunk`'s two throws are decided **ahead of the call**, because the firewall maps `ContentLoadException` to `CNA_RESULT_IO` and would flatten both: an unimplemented codec is `CNA_RESULT_NOT_SUPPORTED` (a caller retries with another codec; it does not fix its numbers) and an over-ceiling declared size is `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, refused before allocating. **Their order is canonical and preserved** — an unsupported codec whose size is also over the ceiling reports the ceiling — as is the quirk that `None` is answered before the ceiling is consulted at all, so a stored chunk looks at neither size. A C reader that reordered any of this would disagree with the C++ one about the same file. Both `CheckedAdd`/`CheckedMultiply` are the same shape in miniature: their canonical `ContentLoadException` would become `CNA_RESULT_IO`, so the condition is mirrored in the C layer to answer `CNA_RESULT_OVERFLOW`, while the canonical function still performs the arithmetic — if the two ever drifted, the canonical throw would fire and the disagreement would surface rather than hide.

**One deliberate deviation from this ABI's own input-string rule, and it is the point of the route.** `CnbLogicalNameProblem` reports *why* a name is illegal, and "is not well-formed UTF-8" is one of its verdicts. Validating the input the way every other string route does would answer `CNA_RESULT_ENCODING` and withhold the answer the caller asked for, so these two routes check only the pointer/length pair. `CnbSmoke.c` asserts the deviation directly — malformed bytes return `CNA_RESULT_SUCCESS` with that exact text — which is the assertion that fails if anyone later "corrects" it. Its neighbour `cna_cnb_asset_type_id_from_name` still validates, and the suite asserts that too, so the difference is pinned rather than looking like an oversight.

**Two mutation checks, because the compression block has an arm this machine does not take.** Breaking `compressed_size < payload_size` moves the exit code to stage 8 by name, which proves the Zstandard round trip executes here rather than being skipped; inverting the support branch makes the codec-absent arm's assertions run and fail, which proves that arm is real rather than dead text. Both were restored. The zstd branch is live in these trees (`CNA_CNB_ZSTD=AUTO` finds libzstd) and the CRC-32C hardware path is in use, so the portable-versus-shipping comparison compares two different implementations rather than one with itself.

**ABI `0.9.0` → `0.10.0`, exports 3,746 → 3,770.** Purely additive, so the four evolution paths cover it; the minor moves because that is what every additive generation since `0.4.0` has done and a consumer needs a number to require. `AbiHeaderC.c`/`AbiHeaderCpp.cpp` freeze the two identity widths, all fourteen asset type values, the container constants, the two chunk identifiers, the flag mask, the seed, and `CNA_CnbReadLimits`'s size, alignment and every offset — its 64-bit members are placed ahead of its 32-bit ones so the layout carries no padding. Its validator uses the documented prefix rule, so `CBIND-100`'s contract is honoured by a structure written after it rather than retrofitted to it.

**`--approve-rule-symbols` could not be used and the approvals were computed instead.** That mode matches with `ignore_approval=True`, which surfaces a pre-existing ambiguity between `graphics-ext-settings-values` and `pbr-material-value` over `CNA::Graphics::PbrMaterial` and aborts before writing anything. The twelve new rules' approvals were therefore derived with the tool's own matcher over the twelve new patterns only, with two assertions in the deriving script: that no two of them claim the same symbol, and that no *existing* rule already approves one. 66 claimed, matching the row count per rule.

**Verified in all three arms**, each built whole and then run serially: 98/98 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug` (HEADLESS, `CNA_CNAEXT=OFF`), `cmake-build-cnaext` (OPENGLES3/EasyGL, `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`) and `build-probe` (HEADLESS, `CNA_CNAEXT=ON`); all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 3,770 in each tree; and the same 3,770 names exported with the engine layer on and off, compared symbol by symbol. `docs/c-api/CNB.md` is the consumer contract, and `CONTENT.md`, `FEATURE_MATRIX.md` and `README.md` now say what a C application can and cannot do with a `.cnb` file — inspect and transform the container, and not load an asset out of it. |
| CBIND-107 | Bind the CNB document and its byte cursors | 88 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** 80 routes over four new handle kinds (`CnbDocument` = 168, `CnbReader` = 169, `CnbByteWriter` = 170, `CnbWriter` = 171) and three new versioned structures close all 88 rows: implemented 7,898 → 7,986 and planned 440 → 352, the delta this phase requires.

**The lifetime question this slice existed to answer, and both halves of the answer.** A cursor points into somebody's bytes, and the two ways of getting one differ in whose:

- **From a document, it borrows.** `cna_cnb_document_open_chunk` retains the document and increments its borrow count, and `cna_cnb_document_destroy` answers `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE` until every such reader is destroyed. Retaining alone would already be memory-safe — the document is immutable, unlike `CBIND-084B`'s pool, which could delete an element out from under a view — so refusing the release is not needed for safety. It is kept because it is this ABI's standing rule for a borrow, and because it turns a leaked cursor into a refused call rather than a document that quietly outlives its handle.
- **From caller bytes, it copies.** The canonical `CnbByteReader` never copies: it documents that the caller must keep the region alive. C has no way to be told that and no way to be caught breaking it, so the adapter owns a copy instead of publishing a lifetime rule it cannot police. This is the slice's one deliberate deviation, and the test proves it rather than asserting it — the caller's buffer is zeroed immediately after the cursor is created and every later read still returns the original value. Mutation-checked: asserting the zeroed value instead fails.

**`ReadString` needs two routes, and the reason generalises.** Reading consumes; copying does not. One route taking a destination could not report a short capacity without either losing the string it had already consumed or consuming it twice, so `cna_cnb_reader_read_string` reads once and reports the size and `cna_cnb_reader_copy_string` hands it over as often as asked. Copying before any read is `CNA_RESULT_INVALID_STATE`, so "nothing read yet" cannot be mistaken for "an empty string". `ReadBytes` deliberately does **not** get the same treatment: its size is the caller's own argument rather than something the file declares, so the capacity is settled before the cursor moves and one route suffices. The test proves that by comparing the position before and after a refused call.

**An out-of-range index is an argument error, and separating it was work rather than style.** Every canonical accessor throws `ContentLoadException` for one, which the firewall maps to `CNA_RESULT_IO` — the answer for a bad file. A caller fixes an index; it does not re-download the asset. `RequireIndex` decides it ahead of the call for all five indexed routes, and a malformed *file* still answers `CNA_RESULT_IO` from the same routes.

**Every throw in this slice is a contract; there are no corrections.** The grep the phase requires came back with 78 throw sites across the four sources and exactly one `std::min`, which is a `reserve` hint rather than a value the reader alters. So nothing here clamps, and every refusal is preserved exactly — including two that had to be decided ahead of the canonical call because the firewall would have flattened them: `CnbWriter::SetCompression`'s `std::invalid_argument` for a codec this build lacks becomes `CNA_RESULT_NOT_SUPPORTED`, matching what `CBIND-106` already decided for the compression routes.

**`SetExternalReferences` becomes clear-then-append.** C cannot pass a table whose entries each carry a string as one argument, so `cna_cnb_writer_add_external_reference` takes the value and the name side by side and `cna_cnb_writer_clear_external_references` empties it. The append hands the accumulated table back to the canonical setter every time, so the writer's own state stays the single source of truth rather than the adapter keeping a shadow copy that could drift from it.

**Five accessors were deliberately not collapsed into an info structure.** `Origin`, `ContainerMajor`, `ContainerMinor`, `AssetTypeId` and `AssetSchemaVersion` each get their own route. The document is immutable, so there is no torn read to prevent and nothing to gain; a `CNA_CnbDocumentInfo` would be a second spelling of five facts a caller can already ask for individually. `CBIND-082` collapsed a video frame's generation and texture into one descriptor for the opposite reason — there, reading them separately could pair a generation with the wrong frame.

**The suite builds a container and parses it back, rather than shipping a fixture.** A fixture proves only that the reader still reads it; a round trip proves the writer still produces something the reader accepts, which is the invariant the format rests on. It also lets the test recompute a chunk's stored checksum with `CBIND-106`'s `cna_cnb_crc32c` from the image bytes — the two halves of the family agreeing rather than each agreeing with itself. Two mutation checks: asserting success instead of the borrow refusal fails at stage 4, and asserting the zeroed source byte fails at stage 2.

**ABI `0.10.0` → `0.11.0`, exports 3,770 → 3,850**, purely additive. `AbiHeaderC.c`/`AbiHeaderCpp.cpp` freeze all three new structures' sizes, alignments and every offset; each places its 64-bit members ahead of its 32-bit ones so the layouts carry no padding. `--approve-rule-symbols` was again unusable for the reason `CBIND-106` recorded, so the fourteen new rules' approvals were derived with the tool's own matcher over those fourteen patterns only, with the same two assertions in the deriving script.

**Verified in all three arms**, each built whole and then run serially: 99/99 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe`; all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 3,850 in each tree; and the same 3,850 names exported with the engine layer on and off, compared symbol by symbol. `docs/c-api/CNB.md` gains the document, cursor and writer contract and the table of which result code means whose mistake. |
| CBIND-108 | Bind the CNB texture schemas | 67 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** 28 routes, one identity with 27 frozen wire values, one new handle kind (`CnbTextureData` = 172) and one versioned structure close all 67 rows: implemented 7,986 → 8,053 and planned 352 → 285.

**The format numbering is the whole point of the first header, and the C side had to not undo it.** `CnbTextureFormat` exists *instead of* serializing `SurfaceFormat`, because `SurfaceFormat`'s enumerators carry no explicit values and are numbered by position: inserting one — an ordinary thing to do to an XNA-shaped enum — would renumber everything after it and silently change the meaning of every `.cnb` already written. Publishing the C constants at anything but the canonical values would reintroduce exactly that hazard one layer out, so `AbiHeaderC.c` freezes **each of the 27 individually** rather than only the first and the last: a renumbering that shifted the middle would pass a maximum check.

**The mapping is round-tripped over all 27, and that is the check that would find drift.** `to_surface_format` then `from_surface_format` must return the identifier it started from, for every one. Mutation-checked by asserting the wrong identifier comes back, which fails.

**Two things a round trip cannot see, so they are asserted directly.** A block-compressed level rounds each dimension up to a whole 4-texel block, so a 1x1 BC7 level is a full block and a 5x4 BC1 level is two — asserted against each format's own reported unit size rather than a transcribed constant, and mutation-checked by asserting a quarter block. And **every identifier decodes while schema 1 encodes `Rgba8` only**: that asymmetry is canonical and is pinned, because the alternative is writing a file no reader of this schema would accept.

**`CnbTextureData` is a handle because it is nested vectors, and the shape of the C form follows from that.** `CnbTextureRepresentation`'s `format` and `levels` have no POD form, so they become `_get_representation_format`, `_get_level_count`, `_set_level` and `_copy_level`; `add_representation` sizes the new representation for the `faceCount * mipCount` the description already declares, so a caller fills levels **by index** rather than pushing them in an order the format would then have to trust.

**`SelectCnbTextureRepresentation` takes a predicate, so it becomes a callback** — a function pointer plus an opaque context, called synchronously, once per representation in order, never retained. The canonical function reports "none supported" by returning the list size; in C that is a sentinel a caller has to know to compare against, so it becomes the availability pair every optional query here uses, with the index left untouched when nothing matched.

**The embedded-atlas pair is named after the objects a C caller already holds** — `cna_cnb_writer_append_embedded_texture2d` and `cna_cnb_document_read_embedded_texture2d` — rather than after the free functions, and its test round-trips an atlas through a **sprite-font** document, so the path is proved against a file the texture decoders themselves refuse. That is the point of embedding: an atlas belongs to one font, unlike a model's textures, which are shared and go through `XREF`.

**Every throw is a contract again; the grep found no clamps at all** across both sources — 34 refusal sites, zero `clamp`/`min`/`max`. The one place a C caller could have been given a softer answer is an out-of-range representation or level index, and that follows `CBIND-107`'s rule: an index is an argument (`CNA_RESULT_INVALID_ARGUMENT`), a malformed file is content (`CNA_RESULT_IO`), checked at both levels of nesting.

**ABI `0.11.0` → `0.12.0`, exports 3,850 → 3,878**, purely additive. Verified in all three arms, each built whole and then run serially: 100/100 `CApi` tests in `cmake-build-debug`, `cmake-build-cnaext` and `build-probe`; all eight build-free gates green; declared and exported routes agreeing exactly at 3,878 in each tree; and the same 3,878 names with the engine layer on and off, compared symbol by symbol. |
| CBIND-109 | Bind the CNB model schema | 129 | ✅ | **Done 2026-08-28.** 71 routes, five identities, two new handle kinds (`CnbModelData` = 173, `CnbModelFromCnj` = 174), three unversioned value structures and eight versioned ones close all 129 rows: implemented 8,053 → 8,182 and planned 285 → 156.

**The shape question this row demanded was answered first, and it went to indexed accessors rather than a handle per node.** Three reasons, and the third is the one that decided it: a bone or a part has no lifetime of its own, so a handle for each would be a lifetime invented by the binding; a handle per node multiplies the registry by the size of the model and hands a caller one release per node to get right; and every cross-reference the *format itself* writes is already an index — a mesh's `partIndices`, a bone's `parent`, the skeleton's `hierarchy`. Indexed accessors keep the C shape and the file's shape the same. 105 rows then followed it without a second decision.

**The material carries two different texture orderings, and confusing them is the one defect a round trip cannot catch — both halves would be wrong together.** Eight *named* slots (CNA's own effect slots, including `DualTextureEffect`'s second layer, which glTF has no counterpart for) against seven per-slot arrays in the importer's order. They are given two deliberately separate index spaces: `CNA_CnbMaterialTextureSlot` for the names, a plain index below `CNA_CNB_TEXTURE_SLOT_COUNT` for `textureCoordinateSets`, `textureTransforms` and `samplers`. `CnbModelSmoke.c` writes a distinguishable value into **all fifteen** and reads all fifteen back after an encode/decode cycle, which is what makes a crossed binding a mismatch instead of a quiet pass.

**Collapsing eight named string fields into one slot-selected route is the `CBIND-035` judgement, applied twice more.** Sixteen count/copy routes would be the same call written out eight times; the same reasoning turns a morph target's three delta vectors, a weight key's three float vectors and the skeleton's three matrix arrays into one route each with a selector identity. What was *not* collapsed is the pair of file lists `BuildCnbModelFromCnj` returns: `absorbedFiles` and `externalReferences` answer different questions for a build system, and merging them would lose that.
